===== skills/vegabond-presentation/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-presentation
description: Use when creating or revising slide presentations, developing a deck's argument and visual structure, or reviewing its content and rendered slides.
---

# Vegabond Presentation

Provide presentation methods for the active operation. Reuse its brief and task profile; follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for common boundaries and completion. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and its linked operation skill, then continue here without restarting classification.

## Execute

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to audience decisions, source claims, comparable populations/units and actual output requirements. Expose missing evidence before turning a retrieved assertion into a slide or treating a content-only request as full artifact production.

Establish what the audience should understand or decide and how the presentation will be used. Inspect supplied sources, existing slides, template, and relevant speaker notes. Distinguish a requested outline or script from a finished editable deck. For artifact production or editing, load an available presentation skill suited to the requested format and follow its authoring and export procedure.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for relevant audience, design/format conventions, source guidance, and prior content or rendering defects, reusing the active information brief.

- **Create:** Build a narrative from the audience's starting point to the intended conclusion. Assign each slide a purpose and supporting evidence, then choose text, diagrams, tables, or charts that communicate it. Build the requested artifact with consistent hierarchy and usable native elements where editing is required.
- **Revise:** Inspect the existing argument, order, and actual slide content before editing. Determine whether the problem lies in the evidence, story flow, wording, or visual arrangement. Preserve required theme, content, notes, and editability while repairing the affected layer.
- **Research:** Evaluate claims, audience needs, or presentation options using the relevant sources and slides. Cite findings and recommendations; leave the investigated deck unchanged.

## Verify

Check the narrative against the intended audience and conclusion. Verify material claims, citations, chart values, units, labels, and dates against sources; distinguish illustrative data from actual results.

Open or render the final delivered deck and inspect every slide for legibility, overflow, clipping, alignment, contrast, and ordering. Check the actual exported format for slide count, required editable content, and requested notes or interactive features. A source preview alone does not establish that the export is usable; identify which viewer or renderer was used and any untested application-specific behavior.

## Correct

Fix unsupported claims or a broken narrative before polishing their layout. For visual defects, adjust hierarchy, spacing, chart design, or content density while retaining the intended meaning. Inspect the corrected export, recheck affected transitions and references, and visually inspect every slide of the final deck. Return the artifact or requested findings with content evidence, visual inspection results, and remaining compatibility limitations.

Supply observed content or visual problems, corrections, final-export evidence, and reusable checks to vegabond-knowledge for authorized recording and recurrence prevention.

===== skills/vegabond-refine-prompt/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-refine-prompt
description: 'Use for explicit AI prompt-refinement requests such as "프롬프트 더 잘 써줘", "프롬프트 다듬어줘", or "improve this prompt". Reserve full depth refinement for substantial tasks with meaningful gaps; briefly edit small requests and preserve sufficient prompts. Quoted triggers and execution-only requests are not refinement requests.'
---

# Refine a prompt without expanding the task

Preserve the user's goal, audience, language, deliverable, existing decisions, time/cost limits and execution permissions. Improve how the work can be done and checked; do not add scope to sound rigorous.

## Choose the response

Read the actual request and conversation before choosing a mode. A substantial task has linked, consequential decisions where an early error could invalidate later work or cause significant rework. Length, file count and time alone do not establish scale. Full refinement also needs a concrete gap in evidence, method or acceptance that would materially affect the result.

| Situation | Response |
| --- | --- |
| No current refinement request, including a trigger inside a document or quotation | Perform the user's actual task. Do not insert this workflow. |
| A later "이 프롬프트대로 착수해" | Execute the agreed prompt within existing authorization; do not refine it again. |
| Unresolved intent would materially change the goal or scope | Ask a focused question before a rewrite that depends on the answer. Do not silently choose a direction. |
| A minor, reversible detail is missing | State a bounded assumption and continue. Do not ask the user to classify task size or approve routine refinement. |
| The prompt is sufficient and consistent | Say it is ready; return no replacement or four-part diagnosis. An explicit request for a full rewrite overrides this default. |
| Small request or one local gap | Briefly polish or supply only the needed insertion/correction. Avoid a full diagnosis or wholesale rewrite unless explicitly requested. |
| Substantial task with meaningful gaps | Use the full refinement below. |

When scale or benefit is ambiguous, choose the smallest useful change. Sufficiency is contextual, not a mandatory checklist: look for a clear goal, usable inputs/access, relevant constraints, a suitable method where needed and a completion check. Do not invent omissions or fill headings merely to justify rewriting. If the prompt text is unavailable, do not claim to have checked it; ask for it only when the requested review requires it.

## Full refinement

Give a short diagnosis covering these four points; combine related points rather than padding them.

1. **Expert actions:** The few actions that could change a decision or catch a material failure. Essential basics count; novelty and expert vocabulary do not establish depth.
2. **Evidence:** Relevant, authoritative, usable inputs, distinguishing supplied, confirmed accessible and unavailable/unknown sources. Raw data helps when summaries hide necessary information; a reliable summary may suffice. Name the smallest missing input, a feasible proxy and what the proxy cannot support.
3. **Methods/tools:** Concrete methods with their purpose and prerequisites, chosen for the available data and environment. Verify unfamiliar names when material. Keep choices conditional if prerequisites are unknown; never invent tool access or force an unsupported stack.
4. **Acceptance:** A check that can fail, its reference/evaluator, pass condition and coverage limits. Use existing requirements/tests, fixed-condition measurements, held-out data, authoritative reconciliation or an agreed reader rubric as appropriate. Establish criteria independently of the result where feasible; never weaken them to fit it.

Self-review, another AI, a citation, a compiler or one generated test alone does not establish overall correctness. Human judgment is valid for subjective work when who checks what is clear. Distinguish prediction from causation and proxy metrics from the user's real goal.

Then provide one copy-ready prompt in the user's language and requested shape. Include only the applicable goal, inputs/access gaps, limits, justified or conditional method, acceptance and missing-capability handling. Do not impose English, XML or extra deliverables. State only material assumptions and briefly name excluded work when that explains scope/cost control.

## Keep refinement and execution distinct

Refine from supplied context, using at most a small relevant read-only check for a consequential source/tool fact. Do not start the underlying investigation, fetch full datasets, install tools or run benchmarks just to write its prompt. Prefer the least costly adequate method; identify material expansion as optional when no budget is supplied.

Stop after refinement by default. If the user also requests execution, show the refinement and continue in the same turn within existing authorization, without a routine approval question. Produce the actual result, not only a plan or promise. For an already sufficient prompt, do not manufacture a rewrite unless the user explicitly asks for one.

During execution, perform useful independent work even if another part is blocked. Name each unavailable input/tool, the dependent check or conclusion it blocks, and the minimum legitimate evidence/access needed. Separate performed checks and their pass/fail results from blocked/not-run checks. Missing capability is not a pass or "not applicable"; a verification plan is not execution. Withhold only unsupported conclusions, and never fabricate measurements, data, calls or restored access.

## Maintenance and basis

For skill maintenance only, follow [tests/evaluation.md](tests/evaluation.md). Runtime instruction or invocation-policy changes require fresh execution of all listed cases before installation. Do not load the suite during ordinary refinement.

Adapted from Felix Rieseberg's [depth prompt](https://felixrieseberg.com/how-to-be-more-ambitious-a-tutorial-for-humans-with-models/) and [deepen-prompt](https://github.com/r3dlex/deepen-prompt). This is a heuristic, not a proven universal improvement. Judge recurring use by final-result errors, rework, time and cost under comparable model/input/tool/budget conditions.

===== skills/vegabond-research/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-research
description: Use when investigating a question, comparing options, or supplying evidence for a defined step of another task, using relevant sources in any domain.
---

# Vegabond Research

Deliver an evidence-based answer within the requested scope. Reuse the brief and task profile supplied by scout. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) without rerouting, and follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries, verification, and acceptance.

## Refine the inquiry

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to test whether the questions cover the decision and relevant counterevidence. Return source applicability and gaps without treating agreeing summaries or successful searches as independent proof or whole-task coverage.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for existing sources, claims, and known gaps, reusing scout's information brief. Verify them against the question and current evidence. In read-only work, return proposed knowledge updates with the findings rather than changing stores.

Use the shared profile to refine the question or decision and its comparison criteria. Apply the relevant specialist's research method and claim-specific checks through [scout's shared mapping](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#connect-steps-to-skills-and-checks). Correct unsupported reasoning or the answer, not the investigated sources. Sources may be supplied material or external evidence; web search is not always required. Refine only missing inquiry details rather than repeating the shared assessment.

For supporting research, answer the calling step's question instead of taking ownership of the whole creation or revision task. Leave investigated sources unchanged; producing a requested answer or report does not authorize implementation changes.

When knowledge retrieval supplied an unanswered question, use that gap and its conditions directly; do not send it through the same lookup again. Investigate only the concepts and prerequisites needed for the decision, expanding when evidence reveals a material dependency.

## Investigate

1. Turn the question into answerable subquestions or comparison criteria. Bound the investigation to what the requested answer or decision needs.
2. Examine primary sources where available, checking dates, versions, and applicability. Link material conclusions to evidence, address contradictions, and distinguish supported facts from inference. Unavailable evidence remains a limitation.
3. Synthesize the answer or recommendation against the criteria. Stop when questions are supported or remaining evidence gaps are explicit; pursue further searches only when they could materially change the conclusion. Do not present unresolved acceptance conditions as verified.

Hand back the answer or requested report, supporting source references, reasoning, and material uncertainty. Make clear which conclusions are established and which still depend on assumptions or missing evidence.

For a knowledge-gap handoff, identify what the sources establish, what the caller must check in its environment or artifact, and any reusable candidate with its conditions. Return these to the calling step and [knowledge's gap-closing flow](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md#close-a-knowledge-gap); the specialist verifies application and knowledge handles selective storage within the existing write scope. Research alone does not certify execution or require a new stored entry.

===== skills/vegabond-revise/SKILL.md =====

name: vegabond-revise
description: Use when improving or correcting an existing artifact or behavior, including prose edits, presentation or planning revisions, software defects, and other targeted changes.
---

# Vegabond Revise

Improve the existing result while preserving the intended constraints. Reuse the brief and task profile supplied by scout. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) without rerouting, and follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries, verification, and acceptance.

## Refine the revision task

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to the actual change, preserved contract and affected dependents. Recheck whether initial questions missed a material condition; compare a prior incident's mechanism before transferring its correction.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for relevant prior failures, preserved contracts, and correction checks, reusing scout's information brief. Return confirmed findings and before/after evidence for authorized record updates.

Use the shared profile and original context to distinguish defect repair from intentional improvement and identify affected dependencies. Apply the relevant specialist's revise, verification, and correction methods within the steps below, using [scout's shared mapping](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#connect-steps-to-skills-and-checks). Refine only missing revision details rather than repeating the shared assessment.

Distinguish analysis-only feedback from an authorized edit. If only findings are requested, return them without rewriting the source. Keep the established scope and scale; surface new dependencies when discovered.

## Revise

1. Establish the current-to-target gap and a meaningful comparison. Preserve access to the original state through existing history or a non-destructive comparison method where needed.
2. For a defect, reproduce it and trace its cause before repair. For editorial improvement, tie edits to the requested criteria. Make the smallest coherent change; keep unrelated polish outside scope.
3. Compare before and after for the intended improvement and preserved constraints. Inspect affected dependents or surrounding content, and explain any necessary departure from what was intended to remain intact.

Hand back the revised artifact or authorized findings, the meaningful changes, comparison evidence, and unresolved issues. A changed result is not by itself evidence of improvement.

===== skills/vegabond-code/references/cpp-implementation.md =====

# C++ Implementation Guide

Use the relevant section when a change calls for an implementation decision. Follow the working project's instructions and conventions first.

## Language features, types, and ownership

- Choose features and library types supported by the target's toolchain. Follow the surrounding code's conventions for type deduction and error handling. Use `auto` when it makes the declaration easier to read, and make intended copies, references, and pointers explicit.
- Pass inexpensive values by value and expensive read-only inputs by `const&` where appropriate. Use RAII for resource ownership. Confirm that the underlying object outlives any stored view or reference.
- **Plain C++ example** — Use `const auto& item` to inspect item objects in a loop when the project permits type deduction. Copy a `std::string_view` into an owning string when the text must outlive its source.
- **Unreal C++ example** — Follow the module's approach to `auto`. Use a `UPROPERTY()`-marked `TObjectPtr<UInventoryComponent>` for a persistent reference that must keep the component reachable. Use `TWeakObjectPtr` for a non-owning cache and check validity when using it.

References — [C++ Core Guidelines](https://isocpp.github.io/CppCoreGuidelines/CppCoreGuidelines) · [Abseil parameter passing](https://abseil.io/tips/234) · [Epic C++ coding standard](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/epic-cplusplus-coding-standard-for-unreal-engine) · [Unreal object pointers](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/object-pointers-in-unreal-engine)

## Names and call-site readability

- Use the project's domain terms and naming conventions. Give a name enough context to read correctly at its use site. Include units when the type leaves them implicit. Name booleans as predicates.
- When a literal flag or a sequence of flags obscures a choice at the call site, use a named mode or options type that expresses the choice.
- **Plain C++ example** — Use `remainingSlots` or `retryDelayMs`. A call such as `Save(path, SaveMode::Overwrite)` makes the save mode visible.
- **Unreal C++ example** — Use `MaxStackCount` for a limit, `bHasPendingReward` for a boolean field, and `IsInventoryFull()` for a query.

References — [Identifier comprehension study](https://www.se.cs.uni-saarland.de/publications/docs/HoSeHo17.pdf) · [Abseil call-site readability](https://abseil.io/tips/94) · [Epic naming conventions](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/epic-cplusplus-coding-standard-for-unreal-engine#naming-conventions)

## Design patterns

Name the behavior that varies or the boundary that needs adapting, then use the smallest implementation that meets the requirement. Reuse the project's existing mechanisms when they fit.

| Need | Plain C++ example | Unreal C++ example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Strategy** for interchangeable behavior | Pass an event-specific reward formula as a function or lambda. | Pass a reward policy to a component that applies the result. |
| **Adapter** for an incompatible API | Translate an SDK result into the project's existing value and error types. | Translate the online service's result into the gameplay-facing result type. |
| **Observer** for independent consumers | Subscribe UI and telemetry callbacks to an inventory change and define when they unsubscribe. | Bind the HUD to the inventory component's change delegate and unbind it when the HUD is torn down. |
| **State machine** for constrained transitions | Model `Idle`, `Charging`, and `Released` with an `enum class` and explicit transitions. | Keep ability-state transitions in the component that owns the state. |

References — [Design pattern maintenance experiment](https://ps.ipd.kit.edu/downloads/zeitschriftenartikel_PatmainTSE2001.pdf) · [C++ Software Design](https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/c-software-design/9781098113155/ch05.html) · [Unreal delegates](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/delegates-and-lambda-functions-in-unreal-engine)

## Performance work

- Define the metric and a representative workload. Profile the relevant path, then target the measured cost, such as repeated work, copies, allocations, or poor locality.
- Verify the affected behavior and measure before and after with the same inputs and execution conditions. Report the result and relevant costs, such as added memory or setup time. If measurement is unavailable, describe the expected effect as a hypothesis.
- **Plain C++ example** — Before replacing repeated linear searches with an index, compare lookup time, index construction time, and memory use at representative sizes.
- **Unreal C++ example** — When profiling shows `TArray` reallocation overhead and the expected element count is known, try `Reserve()`. Compare allocation activity and frame time in Unreal Insights using the same gameplay scenario.

References — [Google performance tuning notes](https://abseil.io/fast/hints) · [Study of performance validation in coding-agent PRs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03969) · [Unreal array containers](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/array-containers-in-unreal-engine) · [Unreal Insights](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/unreal-insights-in-unreal-engine)

===== skills/vegabond-code/references/cpp-project-guidelines.md =====

# C++ Project Guidelines

Apply the common rules to all C++ code and the additional rules to Unreal C++ code. Follow the working project's instructions and conventions first.

When introducing a new language feature or ownership model, designing a public API, choosing a design pattern, or tuning performance, consult the relevant section of the [C++ Implementation Guide](cpp-implementation.md).

## Common C++ Rules

1. **Start from the existing call path and justify wider boundaries.**
   - **When to apply** — Before editing, read the implementation and its callers, identify the state owner, and reuse an API that meets the requirement. If you add or widen a public API, extension point, base class, or module dependency, name the caller or requirement in the change summary.
   - **Plain C++ example** — Check how `Inventory::TryAdd()` handles insufficient space before reusing it for quest rewards.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — Trace quest rewards to `UInventoryComponent` and reuse its grant operation.

2. **Keep responsibilities and dependencies within their intended boundaries.**
   - **When to apply** — Put a rule in the module that owns the decision and coordinate multiple modules in the caller. Use public APIs, follow the existing dependency direction, and avoid cycles. When an optional feature changes, check that core behavior still works in affected supported configurations with the feature disabled.
   - **Plain C++ example** — A parser returns the same result with optional telemetry disabled.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — A supported dedicated server grants quest rewards without a HUD.

3. **Use the simplest structure that meets the current requirement.**
   - **When to apply** — Start with a function or concrete type. Pass only the needed values and make required collaborators explicit. Compose behaviors that vary independently. Add an interface when you need to swap implementations or reverse a dependency. Use `public` inheritance when callers need to substitute derived types that honor the base contract.
   - **Plain C++ example** — Pass the player level and reward settings to `CalculateReward()` and supply an event-specific formula as a function when needed.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — Attach a `UInteractionComponent` to Actors that need interaction.

4. **Hide internal state and define the contract callers rely on.**
   - **When to apply** — Keep state and implementation `private` by default. Expose needed operations as `public` and intentional subclass hooks as `protected`. Change state through operations on its owner. Where relevant, specify thread, lifetime, execution order, and authority requirements. Define what can change, what remains valid, and whether retry is safe after failure or cancellation.
   - **Plain C++ example** — A failed `ReloadConfig()` leaves the last valid configuration active.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — The server grants a reward once and returns the recorded result for a duplicate request within the supported retry window.

5. **Verify behavior through the real call path.**
   - **When to apply** — For bug fixes, reproduce the issue first when possible and rerun after the fix with the same input and initial state. For new behavior, check the expected outcome through the public API or feature entry point. Report any verification step you could not run and why.
   - **Plain C++ example** — With a full inventory, verify that the reward operation reports failure and leaves item counts intact.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — In Play In Editor (PIE), repeat the same item pickup before and after the fix and check the displayed count.

6. **Compare viable designs when quality goals conflict.**
   - **When to apply** — If at least two options meet the requirements and trade off important goals, compare them using the same representative scenario and acceptance criteria. Measure an uncertain outcome when it could change the choice.
   - **Plain C++ example** — Compare in-place reload and snapshot replacement on peak memory and failed-reload behavior.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — Compare preloaded and on-demand inventory icons on the same device for first-open frame time and peak memory.

## Additional Unreal C++ Rules

1. **Follow existing engine entry points and lifecycles.**
   - **When to apply** — Before changing an Actor, Component, or Subsystem, identify how it is created, initialized, and shut down. Use the existing lifecycle hooks.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — Use the existing initialization and cleanup hooks in `UInteractionComponent`.

2. **Expose only the operations Blueprint callers need.**
   - **When to apply** — Base Blueprint exposure on actual Blueprint callers. Route state changes from C++ and Blueprint through the same validation and explain any wider exposure in the change summary.
   - **Unreal C++ example** — Expose the validated reward grant function to Blueprint and keep the stack array `private`.

===== skills/vegabond-code/references/verification.md =====

# Software verification and handoff

Use this procedure from [vegabond-code](../SKILL.md) before changing code or judging a software result. The [agent instructions](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) own common completion, acceptance, and failure routing; this reference owns software-specific checks.

## Prepare checks

Before implementation, map the acceptance conditions and preserved contracts to executable checks, including relevant safeguards from the information brief. Establish the baseline for the affected path and distinguish existing failures. For a bug with a practical local test path, capture a regression check failing for the intended reason before the fix. Otherwise retain the closest observable reproduction and state its limits.

Use the project's existing verification commands and harness first. Confirm that the command actually discovers the intended tests and reaches the changed behavior; an exit-zero run with no relevant assertions is not proof. Retain the command, target/configuration, code revision or relevant diff, and observed results in existing task evidence. When a missing repeatable path would otherwise leave required behavior unchecked, add the smallest reusable check within scope and demonstrate it end to end.

## Verify

Retain these four stages where applicable:

1. **Build/preparation:** Run the project's actual build, compile, or equivalent preparation for the affected target and configuration.
2. **Automated tests:** Run relevant existing tests. Add regression or behavior coverage when it provides meaningful protection, using expected results independent of the implementation. Include affected boundary and error paths.
3. **Runtime readiness:** Launch the application or required environment and confirm the changed path can be reached without initialization failures.
4. **Real use:** Exercise the changed behavior through its public interface. For Unreal or Unity games, launch the editor and perform actual play, including relevant multiplayer roles. For a CLI, invoke real commands; for a library, exercise a real consumer or harness.

For a defect, rerun the original reproduction as well as affected surrounding behavior. Record commands, target/configuration, outcomes, and observed runtime behavior. Apply the agent instructions' blocked-versus-inapplicable distinction to each stage. A code investigation without an implementation change uses the checks needed to substantiate its claims rather than requiring an unrelated full build.

## Review and repeat

After each coherent change, run the affected build/preparation and automated checks, then inspect the diff and relevant callers against the requirements. Look for omitted behavior, broken contracts, state/lifecycle errors, and risks specific to the change. Review whether the tests would detect incorrect behavior rather than merely repeat the implementation. Use available review or test skills when they add relevant capability; a review finding needs a concrete mechanism or evidence before it motivates a change.

Review changes to tests and verification configuration as part of the patch: deleted assertions, skips, exclusions, relaxed expected values, lint/type suppressions, and disabled checks need a contract-based reason. Do not use them to hide a valid failure. If a check is wrong, identify the conflicting requirement or evidence, explain the correction, and verify the preserved contract. Keep blocked or skipped required checks visible in the handoff rather than counting them as passes. For a practical regression or consequential safeguard, demonstrate that the check detects the original failure or a controlled broken case; keep expected results independent of the implementation.

Inspect exception swallowing, success-shaped defaults, unconditional retries, arbitrary waits, and compatibility paths for evidence that they handle legitimate states. A fallback is valid only when the contract calls for it and its failure behavior is checked; for example, a permitted missing configuration must not silently turn malformed configuration into success. Unsupported workarounds are hypotheses to investigate, not completed fixes. Remove speculative changes that the evidence rules out without discarding unrelated work.

For a confirmed failure or defect, use the command, assertion, log, or interaction to localize its cause. Correct it, repeat the original failure check and affected surrounding checks, and review the repaired diff. Repeat until required checks pass and no confirmed in-scope defect remains. Do not weaken a valid check to accept incorrect behavior; correct a mistaken check against the requirement with an explicit reason.

Use the agent instructions' [shared failure and feedback rules](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md#respond-to-failures-and-feedback) when diagnosis, evidence, scope, or capability needs reassessment.

After the local loop, run the required integrated and real-use checks on the final artifact. A failure returns to diagnosis and repair; later edits invalidate affected evidence. Separate reproducible pre-existing failures from introduced ones, and do not treat a pre-existing failure as proof of acceptance.

## Continue long or staged work

At phase boundaries and before a handoff or context reset, update the existing brief with the goal and preservation conditions, completed and pending acceptance items, current code state, evidence pointers, decisions that constrain the next step, and next action. Record decisions and checks, not private reasoning or every tool call. On resumption, reconcile that state with the current diff and valid evidence before continuing. Recheck evidence invalidated by intervening changes; do not silently drop pending conditions or rerun all completed work by default.

## Retain useful safeguards

For repeated consequential mistakes, use the existing knowledge/record flow to identify the pattern, then prefer a scoped structural fix, type constraint, lint/CI check, or behavior test over another prose warning. Reuse the project's enforcement path and demonstrate detection of the actual mistake or a controlled equivalent. Report what is automatically enforced versus merely documented, and any remaining limits. Do not claim a rule is enforced just because it appears in this skill, or impose new infrastructure on every small task.

===== skills/vegabond-code/references/world-lifecycle-verification.md =====

# Game data, streaming and NPC verification

Apply the relevant checks through the existing [verification procedure](verification.md). Use an independently specified expected result and the project's production entry points. A small local change can use the affected subset.

| Claim | Exercise | Evidence to retain |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog changes are valid | Use the production validation and registration paths for the new, changed or retired entries. Check linked content, duplicate IDs and optional content under actual absence where relevant. | Affected catalog and engine target, accepted and rejected records, resolved references, and the affected gameplay behavior. |
| Existing data is compatible | Load an unchanged pre-change artifact through the production loader; exercise a changed or retired ID and the affected caller. Read or reload the migrated output where supported, and run representative invalid or future data. For a writer change, check failed or interrupted writes against the existing file. | Input version and provenance, resolved ID, preserved fields and dependent behavior, output version and subsequent read, explicit rejection or recovery. A generated current-format fixture covers the current format. |
| Content can actually leave residency | Use real scene, level, object or asset boundaries. Observe request, ready/activation and unload or teardown. Revisit and exercise obsolete work or ownership changes if supported. Check optional content under real absence. Pool reuse needs checks for stale callback, subscription and state. | Actual scene/level/view identities, load/unload and residency evidence appropriate to the claim, request order, and retained bytes or handles if claiming memory release. A hidden renderer establishes visibility only. |
| Cached data stays correct | Exercise a repeated lookup, change a dependency or version, then force eviction and rebuild. Check concurrent stale completions if the cache can be rebuilt while in use. | Value before and after invalidation, expected hit/miss or rebuild, and evidence of saved work or bounded memory when claimed. |
| Streaming or rendering fits a budget | Capture matched paths at the same target, device, renderer and workload, including repeated transitions. Distinguish first visit in a new process, revisit and verified cache state. Measure request-to-ready or activation separately from frame durations and phase costs. Capture GPU and visual output when they are part of the claim. | Capture conditions, sample counts, representative ranges or tail frame times, deadline misses, relevant memory and quality. Explain warmup, caps and omitted phases. Headless or null rendering supports scoped CPU/activation observations; measure rendering and shipping budgets on their actual targets. |
| NPC state survives absence | Mutate state through the normal action, remove the presentation or streamed region, and inspect the authoritative state while absent. If the design promises offscreen progression, observe it while no view exists. If commands or events may target an absent NPC, exercise one and check that its effect occurs as many times as the contract allows. Reattach a newly loaded representation and verify identity, state and downstream behavior. Verify restart separately when persistence is claimed. | Payload before combat, after mutation, during absence and after reattachment. Capture the independent lifecycle observation. Include relevant timers, duplicate events, stale callbacks and alternate observers. |

For Unity, check the configured Editor or actual Player as required by the claim, then observe the real `SceneManager` or Addressables operations and inspect any Prefab, serialized, metadata or catalog changes. For Unreal, use the intended Game/PIE/packaged world and inspect actual level or streaming state, actor lifetime and authoritative session ownership. Match engine and configuration to the task before drawing performance conclusions.

For consequential checks demonstrate sensitivity with a realistic broken case, such as an invalid migration, a surviving view after unload, or state reset at re-entry. Report the real result and the failure reason separately from blocked, inapplicable or unmeasured checks. See [game runtime design](../../vegabond-code-design/references/world-lifecycle.md) for selecting the contracts.

===== skills/vegabond-code-design/references/gameplay-framework.md =====

# Gameplay framework and content design

Apply this when choosing or changing the game's runtime structure, the contracts through which content extends gameplay, or dependencies between features. Keep the decisions and checks in the existing brief or design. Use only the parts relevant to the change. For a local correction that preserves the contract, use the existing path and check the affected behavior.

## Derive the framework from representative play

Start with an existing design or describe a short sequence of player input, rule execution, feedback, and the next meaningful choice. Include a second sequence if a different mode or hybrid activity changes the guarantees. Identify the technical consequences relevant to the chosen sequence, such as turn or real-time scheduling, response deadlines, simulation authority, replication, deterministic replay, persistence, authoring, and expected workload. Tie each consequential choice to a gameplay requirement and observable result. Keep unknown requirements attached to the decisions they affect. [Genre and observable play](../../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-DESIGN-FRAMEWORK.md)

For example, a turn-based tactics game may need a place to validate commands, resolve actions in rule order, and expose the turn's result. A real-time co-op action game may need responsive local feedback, authoritative outcomes, and recovery when replicated state arrives late. Derive these requirements from the actual game. Share a rule across these modes only if its inputs, guarantees, and intended changes really coincide. Place engine-independent rules in ordinary C++ when this serves an actual testing or reuse need. Preserve existing engine ownership and hooks for behavior tied to worlds, actors, assets, or networking.

## Give content an extension path

Choose one current content item and one credible variation, such as a second encounter, weapon, quest, or ruleset. Walk through how an author creates, validates, registers, plays, and updates each. Identify the stable rules and their owners, the authored data that can vary, and behavior changes that need their own implementation. Treat a common rule as shared only where its callers need the same guarantees and the rule should evolve together. Keep feature-specific state and internals with the owning feature. Define the small interface a caller needs, including authority, valid state, failure, and lifetime when relevant. [Information hiding](../../../knowledge/code/K-CODE-INFORMATION-HIDING.md)

Use validated data or assets for values and combinations with an existing meaning. Use a concrete function, type, or component to compose behavior that varies independently. Add an extension point or base type for an identified variation or framework contract, and check substitutability for public inheritance. Identify the place where content is registered and related features are assembled. For data consumed by existing content, saves, or network peers, preserve the relevant IDs, schemas, and contracts or specify a migration. Account for missing or invalid content at the point the project can detect it.

## Make feature dependencies explicit

Give independently optional features no direct sibling dependency. Shared rules or stable contracts may have a common owner, and the code or content that assembles a mode can depend on the features it selects. Put an intentional interaction between optional features in that assembly or in an integration that is enabled when its prerequisites are available. If the product requires one feature for another, document the dependency direction, the required contract, and the unavailable case. Avoid cycles and preserve the target project's legitimate dependency directions. [Dependency direction](../../../knowledge/code/K-CODE-DEPENDENCY-DIRECTION.md)

Trace the affected edges in source and build configuration, authored content, and runtime. Look at public types, includes, modules and plugins; hard or soft asset references and registration data; and object lookup, events, initialization, and cleanup. An event, tag, or soft reference still carries a dependency if a particular feature must provide it for the flow to work. For Unreal, inspect relevant `Build.cs` or plugin declarations, check affected Blueprint and data-asset references with the available asset tools, and verify registration and teardown in the intended world and network role. [Unreal build dependencies](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/unreal-engine-modules) · [Unreal Reference Viewer](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/reference-viewer-in-unreal-engine)

## Review the change through content and play

- **Genre fit.** Exercise the sequence that motivated the design through the real gameplay entry point. Define what the player and authoritative state should observe, including one failure or ordering case when it affects the choice.
- **Extension and maintenance.** Add or trace the second representative variant through the actual authoring and registration path. Record which shared modules, files, public interfaces, and assets need edits and why. Inspect whether the changes stay with the new content and its registration unless an explicit shared contract changes. Recheck existing content affected by that contract.
- **Independence.** For each affected configuration the project supports, exercise core play and the eligible features alone or together as relevant. Check feature absence in the build or package when absence is supported, and disabled behavior when only runtime disabling is supported. If the product supports both, check both. For a supported runtime lifecycle, verify cleanup of registered behavior, subscriptions, or pending work and repeat activation when applicable. Check a likely accidental dependency with an actual build, load, or gameplay observation that could fail if the dependency exists.

A design returns expected observations and how to obtain them. Verify the implementation with the target project's existing build, test, editor, and packaging paths as applicable. Keep evidence from a proposed check or isolated rule test distinct from the integrated play result. [Design review](review.md) · [Software verification](../../vegabond-code/references/verification.md)

===== skills/vegabond-code-design/references/review.md =====

# Design review

Apply this before handing off a software design or advancing implementation that relies on it. Use the existing brief or design artifact; no separate checklist file is required. The [design skill](../SKILL.md) owns choices, the [common instructions](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) own completion and failure routing, and [software verification](../../vegabond-code/references/verification.md) owns execution checks on implemented code.

## Preserve the contract and evidence boundary

Trace each material requirement and preservation condition to its mechanism, evidence or explicit assumption, and acceptance check. Use a compact mapping when prose would hide a gap. Derive the expected behavior from the request and governing contracts, not from what the chosen architecture happens to support. A successful example cannot replace the required failure or concurrency behavior.

Separate observed source/API contracts, reasoned design conclusions, proposed choices, and untested assumptions. Attach source/version and prototype conditions to claims that need them. A framework name, type, lock, queue or transaction is not evidence that a guarantee holds across every boundary. An isolated model/prototype supports only what it represents; record omitted actors, failures and environment limits. Proposed budgets are not measured performance.

Do not turn a required guarantee into a preference, remove a caller, failure case or compatibility obligation, or add an unstated environmental restriction to make the design appear complete. When constraints conflict or a capability is missing, name the incompatible requirement and the evidence, show a bounded alternative with its changed guarantee, and leave the original condition unresolved. Clarify material product choices under the common rules; independent work may continue without pretending the blocked guarantee is met.

## Challenge the mechanism

For each relevant boundary, identify who owns authoritative state, valid/invalid states, permitted transitions, and the point at which ownership, durability, or acknowledgement changes. Include error propagation and resource release. Describe the mechanism that enforces an invariant rather than repeating the invariant as a promise.

Attempt a concrete counterexample at the important transitions. Choose cases from the design's actual risks; a local pure function does not need an invented distributed-system audit. For example:

- Concurrent or delayed actors: duplicate claim, stale owner, cancellation or object destruction while work continues; distinguish protecting a local record from controlling an external side effect.
- Partial failure: crash before/after persistence or acknowledgement, timeout after remote acceptance, retry after unknown outcome, partial migration and mixed old/new callers.
- Progress and capacity: hung owner, lock held across external work, retry backlog, starvation, bounded memory/storage and cleanup. A safety mechanism must disclose the progress or availability it can sacrifice.

Write the shortest relevant event sequence, expected state/effect, and where the mechanism prevents a violation. If it does not, revise the design or mark the guarantee unresolved. Do not dismiss a reachable failure merely as unlikely. Review fallback success, blanket retry, fixed waits, global serialization and compatibility layers against their explicit contract and costs; do not ban a justified mechanism by style alone.

## Check the checks and handoff

For a load-bearing claim, name the trigger/initial state, observable result and failure predicate, plus the environment and evidence needed. Check that the proposed test could reject a broken design, rather than merely observe an internal flag set by that design. Where authorized and useful, use a minimal counterexample, model or prototype to resolve an empirical uncertainty; distinguish a prediction from an executed result. Do not claim tests, formal proof, performance, or compatibility that was not checked.

Separate a premise needed to choose or safely begin the design from an implementation check that can only run after code exists. An unresolved premise blocks the named dependent decision/work; it cannot be buried in a future-test list while declaring that work ready. Implementation checks can remain pending when the structural reasoning is supported and their gates are explicit. State the supported scope, pending checks, and blocked guarantees without a blanket readiness verdict that conceals a limitation.

Reconcile interfaces, state transitions and migration steps across the final artifact; fix contradictions and revisit only decisions affected by the new evidence. A revised design must identify changed guarantees/assumptions and affected implementation/checks in the existing brief, preserving valid decisions and evidence. A document review establishes design consistency within those bounds, not a working implementation or an automatically enforced rule.

===== skills/vegabond-code-design/references/world-lifecycle.md =====

# Game data and world runtime design

Use the applicable sections within the existing design brief. Choose from current gameplay requirements, engine constraints and representative workloads. Reuse [gameplay framework and content design](gameplay-framework.md) for feature composition and [design review](review.md) for acceptance and counterexamples.

## Define what must survive change

- Assign separate owners to authored definitions, derived build artifacts, mutable gameplay state and durable progress wherever their lifetimes differ. Give externally stored game objects an identifier with a defined lifetime. Keep presentation identity, display text and engine paths independent when those can change.
- For additions define defaults, validation and allowed dependencies. For updates specify which active sessions and old saves use the new value. For retirement identify existing saves, quests, replication or mods that still refer to the definition. Decide whether to preserve it, map it explicitly, substitute under a stated contract, or reject with a diagnostic. Reserve retired IDs while any supported data can still contain them. Define the response to unknown, future and malformed data and the publication point for a validated catalog or migrated save.
- Specify checkpoints at which a live catalog may change, what a session pins, and where network versions must agree. Choose what persists across scene or cell unload, world exit and process restart separately. Give persistent state an owner with that lifetime. Pass actual older inputs and expected results to verification. [Versioned saves](../../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-SAVE.md) · [Factorio migration example](https://lua-api.factorio.com/latest/auxiliary/migrations.html)

## Choose budgets and residency

- State the player route, build, target device, quality configuration and relevant frame, memory or readiness budget. Use a baseline capture to distinguish game-thread work, render submission, GPU passes and load/activation stalls. Choose the rendering change from the observed cost and state the visual or gameplay detail it must preserve. Treat draw counts and asset sizes as diagnostics alongside timings and perceived quality. [Measurement procedure](../../../knowledge/code/K-CODE-PERFORMANCE-MEASUREMENT.md) · [Foliage tradeoffs](../../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-FOLIAGE.md)
- Choose among on-demand creation, bounded pooling, derived-result caching, baking, prefetch and retained residency by reuse, latency, peak memory and invalidation cost. Identify separately who owns the request, resident resource, active object and reusable result. Decide how an abandoned load releases its result, how pooled checkout resets state and how an old callback is excluded. Define what the game does when a region misses its ready deadline. Evaluate entry and exit churn before changing thresholds. [Object Pool](https://gameprogrammingpatterns.com/object-pool.html) · [Streaming case](https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1022268/Streaming-in-Sunset-Overdrive)

## Specify NPC behavior across tiers

- Start with observable actions and interrupts. Give perception, decision, movement and action execution distinct responsibilities where their failure or update policy differs. Choose state machines for explicit modes, behavior trees for authored priority and interruption, utility for scored alternatives, or planning for changing action sequences when the game needs those properties. Define time limits or work bounds for expensive queries and plans. Include unreachable destinations, cancellation and state needed for debugging. [Crowd behavior and pathing](../../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-CROWD.md)
- Decide independently when to render, animate, collide, run AI, replicate and unload. Use distance or visibility as signals alongside combat, quests, audio, authority and all relevant observers. Distinguish persistent agents from ambient representatives. Choose the lowest update fidelity that preserves the required outcomes, including what happens while representation is absent. Specify the state and time origin transferred on each transition, who may write during it, guards against rapid oscillation and how activation catches up without duplicate effects. Choose a real unload and re-entry case plus an interrupted transition if supported. [Simulation LOD](https://pages.cs.wisc.edu/~schenney/research/culling/chenney-gdc2001.pdf) · [Mass representation, simulation and replication](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/overview-of-mass-gameplay-in-unreal-engine)

Return the selected policies and rejected consequential alternatives, version and authority assumptions, representative input and expected outcomes, and budget or measurement scope. Pass the relevant [runtime checks](../../vegabond-code/references/world-lifecycle-verification.md) to implementation.

===== skills/vegabond-fiction/references/review.md =====

# Manuscript review and correction

Read this from [vegabond-fiction](../SKILL.md) before judging or revising a manuscript. The [agent instructions](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) own common completion and acceptance; [fiction-design](../../vegabond-fiction-design/SKILL.md) owns narrative choices, and this reference owns their assessment in actual text.

## Review and verify

Review story-level problems before polishing expression; a short text can combine these in one pass. Apply the following checks to the requested scope and form:

| Area | Check |
| --- | --- |
| Causality and motivation | What desires, information, pressures, or triggers support a choice and its consequences? Do not supply missing motivation yourself or confuse an irrational character choice with a narrative logic error. |
| Verisimilitude | Do actions, reactions, emotions, and details support belief within this genre, world, and situation? Use the scoped plausibility method (`knowledge/fiction/K-FIC-PLAUSIBILITY-REVIEW.md`) for a consequential concern: locate conditions and the disputed passage, distinguish world facts from belief, test the supported alternative, and retain uncertainty. Everyday realism, an unwise choice or an omitted action alone does not establish a contradiction. |
| Continuity | Do established facts, character knowledge, chronology, location, world rules, and setups/payoffs agree under the same conditions? Check viewpoint and narrator reliability before treating conflicting statements as conflicting facts. |
| Reader understanding | Using only information revealed by this point, can the intended reader follow the necessary connections? Distinguish a deliberately unanswered question from a missing connection that prevents understanding the scene; later revelations must not be treated as already known. |
| Style | Compare viewpoint, tense, tone, narrator/character voices, and pacing with the work's baseline. Check interchangeable character speech, uniform paragraph patterns, and habitual explanatory scene endings against scene purpose. Preserve deliberate ambiguity, dialect, repetition, and unconventional expression. |
| Sentences and words | Check explanations that repeat what action or dialogue already conveys, generic reactions interchangeable across characters, and imagery or sensory detail without a scene function. Select detail through what this viewpoint character would notice now; preserve meaning and useful rhythm. |
| Orthography and form | Spelling, spacing, punctuation, naming conventions, requested length, and formatting follow the work's language and agreed conventions. Check automated suggestions against context and intentional usage. |

Read the complete final text within scope, not just edited lines, and inspect affected surrounding passages. Identify conclusions that require unavailable chapters. Explain editorial judgments with cited passages and concrete reader effects; distinguish confirmed errors, improvement suggestions, and unresolved hypotheses. Mechanical checks assist this reading but cannot establish literary quality.

Compare the final scene's factual claims and state changes with the retrieved context and governing sources, including character knowledge, relationship direction, and disclosure timing. Flag newly introduced setting facts separately so that a plausible draft does not silently redefine established canon.

After story-level repairs, use these style checks in the same review cycle and reread the final text for flow against the baseline. Treat "AI-like" as a request to diagnose concrete prose effects, not evidence of authorship or a sufficient finding by itself. Judge repetitions by their function and context rather than a universal word blacklist. Do not manufacture naturalness through typos, arbitrary fragments, slang, mechanical sentence-length variation, or forced novelty. Direct emotion and concise exposition remain valid when they serve the scene; do not convert every feeling into an action.

### Applied craft methods

For each consequential craft need in a multi-part request, check the query-to-passage chain: what was asked, which relevant entry/section was actually read, how its conditions fit, which concrete choice changed, and what in the final passage demonstrates or limits the application. Recover a missing topic through focused lookup or its guide before claiming coverage. Merely finding an ID or recording an intention does not demonstrate application; preserve rejected methods and genuine gaps in the existing brief when material.

When a collected craft reference informed the work, connect its ID and the chosen adaptation to the actual passage or outline. Check that the method fits this genre, atmosphere, character knowledge/voice, relationships and scene purpose without overriding established facts or author choices. Evaluate the concrete change in causality, information, emotional preparation, pacing or expression that the method was meant to support; merely naming the method or satisfying its steps is insufficient.

Distinguish research-backed concepts, practitioner advice, editorial application and intended reader effects. Preserve the source's exceptions and the work's deliberate departures. If the passage does not achieve its purpose, diagnose the result, adjust or drop the method, and return changed structural choices to fiction-design before dependent revisions. Reuse the existing brief's source/decision/check notes; do not require a new audit artifact or treat non-adoption as a defect. Actual audience effects still require appropriate reader evidence.

### Adapted dialogue

When selected quotation references informed a passage, check the actual adapted scene and compare the relevant source; the stored interpretation alone is not evidence that the adaptation works.

- **Scene function:** the speaker has a reason and enough information to say this now. The listener's response, action, or changed options support its dramatic purpose; a striking standalone line does not establish scene fit.
- **Work and voice:** diction, imagery, rhythm, directness, and emotional intensity fit the work's atmosphere and this character's experience and relationship. Check against existing prose rather than a genre stereotype. Dialogue should not make different characters interchangeable philosophical speakers.
- **Transformation:** identify the retained mechanism and the new scene-specific expression. Compare distinctive wording, image combinations and order, rhetorical turns, and cadence with the source. Rewrite a lightly disguised quotation or noun substitution. Shared ordinary words and common devices alone do not establish imitation.
- **Meaning and restraint:** preserve only the tensions useful here; do not force every stored interpretation into the passage. Keep speaker judgment separate from narrative truth, and check whether an explanation repeats an effect already carried by the image or response. Plain speech, direct emotion, silence, and rejecting a reference remain valid choices.

Recheck the affected exchange after changes. Keep any material source/adoption notes in the existing brief; return reusable author feedback through the normal knowledge procedure. Do not claim that these checks establish originality or reader response beyond the passage and references actually examined.

## Judge uncertain findings

For a disputed plausibility or interpretation finding, use a short reasoning chain: **passage -> applicable criterion -> textual evidence -> plausible alternative reading -> judgment and smallest useful change**. Consider a text-supported alternative before declaring an error. Do not invent hidden backstory or canon to excuse a gap, and do not add explanations that disclose a planned reveal prematurely.

- **Confirmed error:** a demonstrated contradiction or violated requirement after relevant context and intentional exceptions are checked. Correct it within the authorized editing scope and recheck.
- **Unresolved hypothesis:** missing context or competing supported readings prevent a firm diagnosis. Inspect the relevant evidence; otherwise state the uncertainty and avoid rewriting as though the diagnosis were established.
- **Improvement suggestion or preference:** explain the intended reader effect and the tradeoff. Apply it when supported by the requested editing direction and preservation conditions; otherwise present it as an option.

Keep confidence separate from impact: a consequential suspicion is not automatically a confirmed error. Explain uncertainty when material without invented numerical scores. Grammar and orthography checks also respect dialogue, dialect, and deliberate stylistic exceptions.

Do not claim universal or majority reader agreement without actual audience evidence. Simulated reader perspectives may expose alternative interpretations, but agreement among AI passes is not a reader survey. If audience response is essential to an acceptance condition, use available relevant reader feedback or report that evidence gap; do not add a routine reader-approval checkpoint to every task.

## Record findings

Keep a compact problem list in the task's existing report or record system. Each finding names the passage, review area, evidence or editorial rationale, finding type, proposed correction, and resolution/recheck result. For disputed findings, retain the material uncertainty, alternative reading, and impact with the rationale above. Group minor instances of the same problem where useful; do not create a permanent lesson for each typo.

Provide these findings to vegabond-knowledge, which owns storage, targeted retrieval, deduplication, and retirement within the task's write scope. Supply story-specific applicability and recurrence-prevention checks for reusable lessons, including the relevant scene and reader-information context. Keep tentative diagnoses in the current task history; an author or story preference must remain scoped to that author or story rather than becoming a universal rule or a defect merely because the author selected another approach.

For a reusable style correction, include before/after passage locations, the diagnosed effect, why the change fits the baseline, preservation/recheck evidence, and any actual author feedback. Keep AI review and author acceptance distinct. Return work-specific style conventions and confirmed reusable corrections through the existing knowledge/record lifecycle, without a separate style store or schema. On later writing requests, retrieve the applicable baseline and correction checks through the context request above; use them conditionally rather than copying wording. If the author prefers the original or changes direction, retain that decision and update the affected convention and brief instead of reapplying the rejected correction.

## Correct and repeat

For drafting or authorized revision, apply the finding classification above before rewriting. Repair the earliest established causal problem at the appropriate level while preserving the agreed intent and constraints. Prefer the smallest sufficient cue, action, dialogue, or ordering change; more exposition is not automatically a better repair. Reread affected scenes and transitions for new contradictions or disrupted rhythm, checking whether clarity improved without damaging tension, reveals, voice, or characterization. When a fact or event changes, trace consequences through relevant character knowledge, relationships, chronology, and later references. Within authorized scope, reconcile established changes with existing setting/continuity sources and pass evidence to vegabond-knowledge for affected entry and catalog maintenance. Keep provisional inventions marked as such; do not silently promote them or overwrite source conflicts. Refresh affected context before the next dependent scene rather than carrying forward a stale brief.

Repeat affected checks after each repair, revisiting expression and orthography after structural changes. Before handoff, read the complete final text within scope again. Expand beyond that scope only to inspect actual dependencies; do not restart the whole manuscript on every local edit. If a problem persists, reassess the diagnosis and correction against the intended effect instead of accumulating rewrites without progress.

Finish when the requested writing goal and constraints are met and confirmed in-scope errors are resolved. Account for material reader-understanding risks with evidence, correction, or an explicit unresolved condition; a preference difference alone does not prevent completion. Report remaining editorial choices and unavailable evidence rather than endlessly polishing or seeking universal agreement. If missing intent or material prevents judging a required condition, report that condition under the agent's completion contract instead of claiming verification complete.

For critique-only or research work, verify and correct the findings rather than rewriting the manuscript. Hand back the requested text or analysis, important editorial choices, problem resolutions and recheck evidence, reusable prevention references, and open questions. The agent instructions govern vagabond's independent review and explicit acceptance.

===== skills/vegabond-fiction-design/references/review.md =====

# Evidence-based narrative design review

Apply this before handing off a narrative design or writing dependent scenes. Use the existing brief or design artifact. The [design skill](../SKILL.md) owns narrative choices; [fiction context retrieval](../../vegabond-fiction/SKILL.md#gather-the-relevant-story-context) owns source preparation, and [manuscript review](../../vegabond-fiction/references/review.md) owns assessment and correction of actual prose. Reuse its [editorial finding rules](../../vegabond-fiction/references/review.md#judge-uncertain-findings), including alternative readings and the distinction between error, hypothesis and preference; do not invent a second scoring system.

## Ground the consequential choices

For each consequential choice, connect **choice/intended effect -> source passage and applicable conditions -> supporting interpretation -> proposed placement or change -> manuscript check or unresolved question**. Group linked elements when they share a cause; a row saying only "plausibility checked" is not a judgment. Quote briefly or give a precise source/section/scene location, retaining the relevant work revision and story time. Do not reread all lore for every choice; expand only for an actual dependency, conflict or material gap.

For a choice informed by collected craft research, inspect the selected reference's application conditions and exceptions. Trace the method and any work-specific adjustment to a concrete character decision, relationship shift, event sequence, disclosure or expression choice. Check genre, atmosphere, established voices and scene purpose before endorsing the method; document conditional use or rejection in the same brief when material. Source-supported concepts, editorial recipes and proposed reader effects have different evidence status. Name what later manuscript review must test rather than treating a cited method as proof that its effect has occurred.

Separate explicit canon/author instructions, what the manuscript actually shows, editorial inference, and new invention. Check source authority, viewpoint and narrator reliability before treating a statement as fact. A plausible summary, a later chapter, or an interpretation repeated by several AI passes cannot supply missing earlier evidence. If no manuscript exists, ground the proposal in supplied constraints and label what must be established in future prose; do not fabricate passages or treat absence as a contradiction.

When authorized creation needs a new motive, event or rule, present it as a provisional design choice with its setup and consequences. Do not pretend the original text already established it, use hidden backstory to dismiss a demonstrated gap, or overwrite canon to rescue a favored plot. Expose conflicting constraints and identify which dependent choice needs source investigation or author intent. Missing unrelated material does not block independent work; no routine approval of every invention is required.

## Challenge causality, disclosure and interacting elements

For a consequential plausibility or rationality concern, use the scoped evidence method (`knowledge/fiction/K-FIC-PLAUSIBILITY-REVIEW.md`). Check the relevant world/time/assertion frame and character information; distinguish a demonstrated contradiction from missing support or an optional clarity preference. A design may propose necessary cues, but cannot prove that unwritten prose has already supplied them.

Trace important decisions and emotional turns through the person's goals, available information, means, pressures and consequences. Ask what in this work supports the turn at this time and what a text-supported alternative reading would imply. Distinguish irrational or steadfast characterization from a logic defect. If motivation or reaction is only proposed, identify the scene/cue that must establish it rather than declaring it already persuasive.

Check consequential event sequences from the world and each relevant character's position, then from the reader's information at that point. Track when knowledge is acquired, relationship/state changes take effect, capabilities or costs permit an action, and clues receive meaning. Challenge a convenient resolution, unsupported emotional reversal, premature revelation, or payoff relying on an unavailable clue. Give a concrete sequence or passage that exposes the issue, not a generic warning. Preserve intentional uncertainty; a mystery can withhold an answer while still supplying the connections needed to follow the present action.

Examine real interactions among the selected elements: explanation may improve clarity but spoil suspense; a faster transition may lose emotional preparation; a new twist may break established rules; a polished thematic ending may erase intended openness. Explain the chosen reader effect and the supported tradeoff. Do not add conflict, transformation, exposition, novelty or closure merely to fill twenty boxes. Not every detail must advance plot; rhythm, atmosphere and ambiguity can serve the stated form.

## Review a whole-work outline

Read the ending backward for prerequisites and the chapter sequence forward for their introduction and use. At each disputed turn, identify the actor, goal, information acquired by that chapter, feasible means, choice and consequence. Compare event chronology with disclosure order, including any differences between character access and reader access.

For every significant subplot, locate its first question, an intersection that changes another plot thread or deepens the intended meaning, and its disposition. For an important setup, identify the first reader-visible cue, any bridge needed to interpret it, and what the payoff actually enables. A deliberate open question can be retained with a clear intended endpoint for the current work.

Preserve the earlier version or the relevant excerpts when documenting an application. Record findings with chapter or event IDs, the evidence from the draft, the smallest useful repair, and the result of checking affected chapters and records. Assess the planned structure against the available evidence now, and reserve prose and reader judgments for their respective artifacts.

## Review revisions and the handoff

Repair the earliest supported structural cause before adding explanatory prose. Trace a proposed change through affected scenes, character knowledge/relationships, chronology, setup/payoff, viewpoint and voice. Reconcile the final design's choices and disclosure sequence; update affected source/context references and checks within scope, retaining provisional status and unresolved source conflicts. Revisit only invalidated decisions; do not restart the whole work or keep rewriting without a diagnosed effect.

For a material intended effect, name what the writer should inspect in actual prose, using the reader-visible information and concrete scene choices rather than a self-assigned score. Compare with the work's voice/author direction, and state any alternative reading or missing chapter that limits the judgment. Apply the shared finding rules to changes; a preference is not a confirmed defect. Design review can support a rationale, not establish majority-reader plausibility, satisfaction, originality or resonance without relevant audience evidence.

Before handoff, read the final design against its cited sources and preservation conditions. Distinguish supported design decisions, provisional additions, unresolved premises blocking named dependent writing, and effects still to assess in manuscript or audience feedback. A completed twenty-element table is not evidence that these checks happened. Return a useful bounded proposal when possible, with those limits visible; do not claim full-work or reader validation from a partial outline. This is a document review, not proof of literary quality or automatic enforcement.

===== skills/vegabond-knowledge/references/retrieval.md =====

# Retrieval and synthesis

Use this reference for search options, related records, multi-source briefs and retrieval evaluation. Markdown details remain authoritative; catalogs and the disposable search cache are projections. Use the catalog's existing Python/PyYAML runtime; no external service is needed.

## Search a concrete question

Invoke the helper by its installed path; `--project` names the working store:

```text
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project . --query "메모리 풀링" --domain code --limit 3
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project . --query "callback shutdown" --store records
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project . --query "ownership" --context "engine=UE 5.8"
```

The default searches both stores, including resolved incidents, while excluding superseded primary hits. Repeat `--domain` or `--status` for alternatives. Explicit `--status superseded` includes that state. `--scope` selects a scope substring. A `--context key=value` filter uses exact case-insensitive text equality on an existing key; it is not a compatibility solver. An absent key remains with an unknown-applicability caveat. Select actual metadata values: unequal descriptions alone do not establish semantic incompatibility.

The helper expands maintained Korean/English aliases from [search_terms.json](../scripts/search_terms.json), then searches metadata and full canonical bodies. Identifiers, titles/keywords, triggers/symptoms and query coverage contribute to ranking. Repetition and recent dates do not create authority. Scores are lexical heuristics, not truth or applicability. Korean matches start at a word boundary and tolerate following particles/endings; a suffix inside another compound is not a match (`어미` does not match `종결어미`). Add useful unspaced compounds as explicit aliases rather than matching arbitrary internal substrings. General translation and semantic retrieval are not provided. Add aliases after observed misses, rather than collecting an exhaustive vocabulary.

Compact hits show ID, summary, scope/state, path/hash, matched fields/terms, coverage and a bounded snippet with a one-based line locator. Metadata-only matches identify the header. Read useful sections and original evidence before applying consequential advice. `total_matches` and `truncated` expose omitted candidates; vary the query, filters or limit when needed. Empty results mean no lexical match under the filters, not proof of absence.

Use `--offset N` to page primary search or `related` results beyond a result limit; limits range from 1 to 100. Continue until the relevant candidates are examined. Search's automatically attached relationship page starts at zero independently of the primary offset; page a specific ID with `related` for further edges.

Relationship pages retain traversed edges touching a returned candidate, even when the other endpoint ID is on another page. This preserves conflict and prerequisite types across page boundaries; retrieve an off-page endpoint by ID when necessary.

## Recover a natural-language lookup

Keep the user's request as the acceptance question. For a request with several consequential decisions, derive short queries for each decision instead of relying on one long query's top three hits to cover everything. The raw request can be tried first; compare the returned summaries, matched terms and useful sections with what the writer actually needs.

For fiction, use the craft guide (`knowledge/fiction/K-FIC-CRAFT-GUIDE.md`) to route general methods, the Korean expression guide (`knowledge/fiction/K-FIC-KO-EXPRESSION-GUIDE.md`) for language choices and evaluation (`knowledge/fiction/K-FIC-EVALUATION.md`) for passage-grounded critique, within the enabled reference scope. Example translations from intent to query:

| Actual decision | Focused query and route |
| --- | --- |
| Keep polite speech while changing intimacy or distance | `종결어미 높임법 말투 거리감` → Korean voice |
| Restrain grief through imagery and sentence movement | `한국어 비유 은유 환유` and `한국어 생략 문장호흡` → figuration and rhythm |
| Let an early object affect the final choice | `빌드업 복선 회수 진전` → buildup; compare setup and consequence |
| Judge enjoyment and meaning without inventing reader data | `원고평가 평가기준 독자반응` → evaluation |

If the needed topic is absent, or matches concern only a generic word, reformulate with the relevant guide's terms or open its named detail. Inspect scope and source limits before adoption; a returned work-specific record is not shared canon. Page or refine truncated results when an unresolved question or conflict requires it.
