id: "K-GAME-ACCESSIBILITY-ONBOARDING"
title: "Design learnable and accessible interaction across game genres"
summary: "Separate the intended challenge from barriers in perceiving information, understanding objectives and operating controls. Plan redundant cues, adaptable input and revisitable instruction, then verify the complete player journey with relevant users and devices."
kind: "reference"
scope: "Game onboarding, accessibility, controls and difficulty planning across genres; Xbox guidelines are design references rather than proof of compliance"
keywords: ["accessibility", "onboarding", "tutorial", "input", "objective-clarity", "difficulty", "readability", "접근성", "튜토리얼", "온보딩", "조작", "학습", "난이도", "가독성"]
status: "active"
checked_at: "2026-11-07"
context: {"evidence_check": "Named primary source text or slides inspected; source-specific access limits in Evidence", "application_status": "Source-based reference and proposed synthesis; no project gameplay trial"}
---

# Design learnable and accessible interaction across game genres

## Guidance

Source observations: XAG 103 recommends communicating critical information through another sensory channel [S1]. XAG 107 covers remapping and alternatives to demanding input patterns [S2]. XAG 109 addresses reviewable objectives and instruction beyond static control diagrams [S3]. XAG 108 separates experienced difficulty into player capability and game barriers [S4].

Proposed use: map first launch, settings, initial action, failure, return after a break and late-game demands. For each necessary action, list what must be perceived, understood, remembered and performed. Preserve the intended challenge while identifying avoidable sensory, cognitive or motor barriers. Specify how altered bindings, subtitles, cue channels and assistance affect tutorials and the rest of the interface.

Prefer tasks that reveal whether players recognize the current goal and act independently. For discovery-focused games, offer layered or optional help so access to essential interaction need not disclose the puzzle answer.

## Applicability and exceptions

Apply the relevant guideline sections to the actual mechanic and audience; do not impose one difficulty count or erase intended challenge. Local competitive assistance may require fairness review. A high-contrast screenshot, automated check or disabled sensory channel alone cannot establish accessibility for all players. Source videos and game examples were not played in this task.

## Checks

Proposed checks: can players reach settings without first crossing the barrier? Do prompts follow remapped inputs? Can critical cues be understood without color alone or sound alone? Can returning players recover goals without replaying everything? Test the relevant complete journey and report participant/device/settings scope; keep optional hints distinct from mandatory spoilers.

## Evidence

- [S1] Microsoft, [XAG 103: Additional channels for visual and audio cues](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/xbox/accessibility/xbox-accessibility-guidelines/103), Implementation guidelines.
- [S2] Microsoft, [XAG 107: Input](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/xbox/accessibility/xbox-accessibility-guidelines/107), Overview / Implementation guidelines.
- [S3] Microsoft, [XAG 109: Objective clarity](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/xbox/accessibility/xbox-accessibility-guidelines/109), Scoping questions / Implementation guidelines.
- [S4] Microsoft, [XAG 108: Game difficulty options](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/xbox/accessibility/xbox-accessibility-guidelines/108), Overview / Scoping questions.
- Official text sections opened 2026-11-07. The [XAG overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/xbox/accessibility/guidelines) states these are not a legal/compliance checklist. Proposed cross-genre checks have not been validated with participants here.

title: Design combat around readable threats and meaningful responses

kind: reference
scope: Action combat; enemy and ability design; PvE and conditional PvP transfer
keywords: [action, combat, enemy design, counterplay, telegraph, encounter, prototype, playtest, 액션, 전투, 적 설계, 대응, 전조, 전투 기획, 플레이테스트]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Official developer articles read; no gameplay experiment performed
  application_status: Transferable synthesis and proposed checks; untested in a target game
---
# Design combat around readable threats and meaningful responses

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Riot describes counterplay as a player's available response to an enemy threat; intelligible responses support agency and back-and-forth depth. PlatinumGames explains that NINJA GAIDEN 4 increased enemy mobility alongside player mobility while preserving reaction opportunities. Early soldiers allow experimentation, while officers encourage using the new stagger system. These are two different combat contexts, not a shared timing formula.

**Transferable synthesis.** For each enemy action, specify the cue, threatened space, player response, cost, and consequence. Build the repeated loop as perceive → choose → commit → read outcome → reposition. A dodge that always cancels every commitment may erase the decision; an attack with no observable cue may turn learning into guessing. Decide which effect is intended before adjusting damage.

Use enemy combinations to test interactions between spacing, camera, animation, stamina, target selection, and recovery. Introduce a response where players can discover its value, then combine it with another pressure. Keep alternatives visible: a specialist response can be efficient without making every other action useless. This sequencing is a proposed planning method, not a reported result from either developer.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for real-time melee, character action, boss fights, and abilities that invite reactions. Strategic preparation or positional avoidance can provide counterplay when an individual hit is deliberately unreactable. Horror, ambushes, and attrition may intentionally limit immediate agency; state that experience target and inspect the larger recovery loop. PvP advice needs separate latency and skill-bracket checks.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Prototype one threat and two responses before a full encounter. Ask unfamiliar players to identify the cue and explain their loss before explaining the rule. Record whether failure came from recognition, decision, execution, or missing information. Repeat with multiple enemies and the production camera; verify that effects and off-screen attacks preserve the intended response. Compare costly and cheap responses for dominant repetition.

## Evidence

- [Riot, Champion Counterplay](https://www.leagueoflegends.com/en-us/news/dev/quick-gameplay-thoughts-may-14/), “Champion Counterplay,” opening definition and fairness/depth discussion; official article text opened on 2026-11-07. Supports the stated design rationale, not universal player outcomes.
- [PlatinumGames, NG4 Enemy Battle Design](https://www.platinumgames.com/official-blog/article/14074), opening mobility discussion and D.D.O. soldiers/officers paragraphs; official text opened on 2026-11-07. Videos and in-game behavior were not tested; numerical timings are not generalized.

  evidence_check: Two Valve developer slide decks read in relevant cooperation and pacing sections
  application_status: Proposed transferable design; no Director implemented or playtested

# Connect cooperative dependency to bounded adaptive pacing

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Valve's cooperative-design presentation uses enemy incapacitation and rescue opportunities to reinforce team dependency while avoiding arbitrary leashes. The AI presentation describes an intensity estimate for each survivor, using the team's maximum to control build-up, sustain, fade, and relaxation. It explicitly distinguishes pacing from difficulty and exempts boss encounters from this adaptive mechanism.

**Transferable synthesis.** Design the social loop first: notice a teammate's need → accept a personal cost → assist → recover together → advance. Make the help action observable and valuable. Shared presence alone does not establish cooperation; a stronger solo build may otherwise erase the need to communicate or rescue.

Then specify the Director's observation, decision, permitted intervention, and constraints. Distinguish existing threats from new population: suppressing future spawns does not instantly end an active fight. Separate authored beats from adaptive filler and define what remains invariant, such as route goals or boss identity. Unbounded random variation can damage learnability even if it increases encounter count.

Connect the controller to navigation, visibility, spawn validity, resource recovery, music, and objective progress. A relaxation state without a traversable route or recovery opportunity may feel empty rather than relieving. Conversely, healing every difficult moment may remove the consequences that give assistance meaning. These are proposed design implications, not outcomes verified in this task.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for replayable mission-based PvE with shared danger and meaningful recovery. Competitive fairness, tightly choreographed set pieces, speedrun comparability, or intentionally solitary play may require fixed schedules or disclosed constraints. Team-maximum intensity prioritizes the most pressured player but can underchallenge others; choose aggregation for the intended social experience. Gameplay events are imperfect proxies for stress, never direct emotion measurements.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Prototype one rescue dependency before procedural spawning. Compare teams with mixed skill and separated positions. Log observed signals, state transitions, spawn decisions, and veto reasons. Replay identical routes with fixed seeds to diagnose variations. Check whether players can explain why they helped and whether relief arrives after combat actually ends. Compare adaptive and fixed schedules for comprehension and repetition without assuming the adaptive version wins.

## Evidence

- [Valve, Replayable Cooperative Game Design](https://cdn.fastly.steamstatic.com/apps/valve/2009/GDC2009_ReplayableCooperativeGameDesign_Left4Dead.pdf), PDF pp. 14–21, “Requiring Cooperation” and enemy incapacitation/rescue; official slides text opened 2026-11-07. No session video viewed.
- [Valve, The AI Systems of Left 4 Dead](https://cdn.fastly.steamstatic.com/apps/valve/2009/ai_systems_of_l4d_mike_booth.pdf), PDF pp. 79–82 and 92, adaptive pacing algorithm, boss exception, pacing/difficulty distinction; official slide text opened 2026-11-07. Historical algorithm; example durations not generalized.

status: "active"

# Choose genre methods through intended experience and observable play

## Guidance

In the MDA framework, **Mechanics** are the rules, data and algorithms available to the designer. **Dynamics** are the patterns that emerge as those rules interact with player input over time. **Aesthetics** are the feelings or experiences the designer hopes play will evoke [S1]. In a proposed cooperative puzzle, giving each player different clues is a mechanic. Exchanging clues aloud would be an observable dynamic. Whether players feel fellowship is a separate playtest question.

Proposed use: describe the intended experience in concrete terms, then map a playable sequence: available information -> action -> system response -> changed options. Identify what players should be able to learn, express, coordinate, or overcome. Name an observable behavior that would support or undermine that intention. Choose genre references matching the bottleneck: readable response windows for combat, competing allocations for strategy, inference for puzzles, or information pacing for horror.

For a hybrid, identify the primary activity and supporting systems. Check where a supporting reward, timer, resource limit, or disclosure changes the intended experience. Keep the existing project design authoritative and trace decisions through its system documents rather than creating a second design bible.

## Applicability and exceptions

MDA is a conceptual lens, not an empirical law or a required checklist of emotions. Its aesthetics concern player responses, not only graphics. Neither every game nor every moment requires a new reward or difficult choice. Apply source examples within their design context; platform, player group, competitive structure, and accessibility can change the appropriate method.

## Checks

Proposed checks: can a representative sequence demonstrate the intended experience? Are the important choices, information, failure/recovery paths, and feedback visible? Does a local optimization undermine the whole loop? A genre label or coherent diagram does not establish actual enjoyment; record the build, participants, conditions and observation when playtesting becomes authorized.

## Evidence

- [S1] Hunicke, LeBlanc and Zubek, [MDA: A Formal Approach to Game Design and Game Research](https://www.cs.northwestern.edu/~hunicke/MDA.pdf). The original 2026-11-07 review covered “MDA,” “MDA as Lens” and “Aesthetics,” pp. 1–3. The three definitions and the perspectives of designer and player were rechecked on p. 2 on 2026-11-19.
- The workflow and review questions here are a transferable synthesis, not a reported trial. No project game, player response, or commercial effect was tested.

status: "active"

context: {"evidence_check": "Named primary source text or slides inspected; source-specific access limits in Evidence", "application_status": "Source-based reference and proposed synthesis; no project gameplay trial"}

# Model game-resource flows before tuning individual rewards

## Guidance

Source observations: Dormans models interacting resource flows and feedback structures, then tests a simplified strategy game with scripted policies [S1]. Schreiber discusses resource generation, destruction, exchange and the different conditions of open economies [S2]. Unity maps progression-relevant resources to earning and spending activities in an F2P-oriented guide [S3].

Proposed method: define the resource and accounting boundary. For each source and sink, record amount, rate, trigger, cap, recipient and prerequisite. Track transfers separately from creation or destruction: a player trade does not itself remove currency from the whole economy. Start with a balance identity: ending stock = starting stock + created + transferred in - consumed - transferred out.

Compare plausible strategies and periods, including novice, efficient, returning and capped players where relevant. Trace a timing or reward change into progression, purchasing choices, scarce inputs, and content demand. Label model assumptions and compare predictions with authorized playtest or live observations before committing a tuning change.

## Applicability and exceptions

The account boundary may be one player, a faction, a server, or one run; conclusions change with it. Resource models omit information, execution skill and social behavior unless represented. A closed single-player economy and player-traded persistent economy have different goals. Source/sink equality is not universally desirable during growth or depletion. F2P examples do not require premium currencies, energy gates or paid acceleration in another game.

## Checks

Proposed checks: reconcile units and accounting totals; test caps and loops under alternate policies; identify runaway accumulation, starvation and dominant conversions. Report sensitivity and excluded behavior. A simulation result holds for that model and policy, not for all players or a proven stable market.

## Evidence

- [S1] Joris Dormans, [Simulating Mechanics to Study Emergence in Games](https://pure.hva.nl/ws/portalfiles/portal/141880/556168_dormans_simulating_mechanics.pdf), 2011, university repository original paper; Resource Flow / Simulating SimWar / Discussion, PDF pp. 3-7 including repository cover.
- [S2] Ian Schreiber, [Level 10: Final Boss](https://gamebalanceconcepts.wordpress.com/2010/09/08/level-10-final-boss/), 2010, Economic Systems / Open and closed economies / negative-sum discussion. Author original opened.
- [S3] Unity, [Building an in-game economy](https://unity.com/how-to/building-game-economy-guide-part-2), Understanding resources / Mapping your sources and sinks. Official guide opened; F2P framing retained.
- All checked 2026-11-07. No game economy simulation or actual player test was run here. Current Machinations documentation returned incomplete shells and was not used as inspected original evidence.

title: Plan curiosity-driven exploration as questions, observations, and usable discoveries

kind: reference
scope: Game planning; authored curiosity-driven exploration and knowledge-based mystery worlds; separate from ability-gated metroidvania progression
keywords: [exploration, curiosity, knowledge-progression, environmental-storytelling, mystery, 탐험, 호기심, 지식기반진행, 환경서사, 미스터리]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Mobius product description and GDC session abstract inspected; full talk and slide PDF unavailable to this check
  application_status: Reference research only; proposed exploration playtests unexecuted

# Curiosity and usable discoveries

## Guidance

**Source-supported:** Mobius describes Outer Wilds through mysteries, locations that change over time, environmental hazards, and tools for probing, tracking signals, translating, and traversal [1]. Kelsey Beachum's official GDC abstract states the design goal of player-determined, curiosity-driven exploration rather than assigned missions, with a nonlinear story in a changing time-loop world [2]. The abstract establishes that goal and topic; it does not expose the talk's detailed techniques or their results.

**Reusable planning synthesis:** Start with questions a player could actually form after seeing or hearing something. For each discovery, record its location, prerequisites, what the player can observe, what interpretation it permits, and the next investigation it makes attractive or possible. Draw an information graph alongside the spatial map: nearby rooms need not contain consecutive knowledge, and distant clues may explain each other.

Plan several plausible leads around a central mystery, with enough partial answers that investigation produces progress before the final explanation. Decide which links require explicit naming, a recognizable landmark, a signal, or a remembered relationship. Preserve player choice by allowing competing questions; keep a recovery path for someone who misses a lead. A journal can retain observations without automatically solving their interpretation.

Prototype a compact cluster with two leads, a meaningful discovery, and a return that uses the new understanding. If locations change with time, test the information needed to predict access, the cost of waiting or retrying, and what persists after failure. Environmental danger can make observation costly; the resulting pacing should still leave room to inspect and reason. New lore, new scenery, and a discovery that changes action are different content rewards and should be budgeted separately.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for authored mystery exploration where information carries value. Collection-heavy open worlds, survival sandboxes, and tourism experiences may need other motivators. Knowledge cannot be forgotten on a new save, which affects replay and spoiler sensitivity. Do not equate knowledge progression with acquiring a movement ability; use the separate metroidvania entry for that topology problem.

## Checks

Proposed checks: ask first-time players what they are investigating and why; trace each inference to encountered evidence; record stalled leads, unintended spoilers, revisits, and recovery. Observe alternative discovery orders and a return after a break. Check timed access with late arrivals. These are proposed local checks, not reported Outer Wilds experiments.

## Evidence

1. [Mobius Digital, Outer Wilds](https://www.mobiusdigitalgames.com/outer-wilds.html): “Welcome to the Space Program,” “A World That Changes Over Time,” and “Grab Your Hiking Gear”; official descriptive text inspected, embedded videos not inspected.
2. [GDC 2021, Sparking Curiosity-Driven Exploration Through Narrative in Outer Wilds](https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1027368/Independent-Games-Summit-Sparking-Curiosity): “Overview”; abstract only. Linked slide PDF returned HTTP 403; no slide or full-talk claims used.

status: active

context:
  evidence_check: Frictional Games lessons and Motive developer account inspected; distinct design approaches retained
  application_status: Reference research only; fear, fairness, and pacing checks unexecuted
---

# Uncertainty and horror pacing

## Guidance

**Source-supported:** Thomas Grip's Frictional Games retrospective reports that frequent scares, exploitable threats, and absorbing combat or puzzles could undermine the studio's intended experience. It argues for narrative context, a defined player role, imperfect information, and agency [1]. Motive describes a different implementation for Dead Space: its Intensity Director combines environmental events and enemy spawning, monitors intensity, and alternates peaks with breaks. The team reports extensive iteration [2]. These accounts support multiple approaches rather than a prohibition on combat.

**Reusable planning synthesis:** State what the player should fear, why their character must engage, and which actions remain possible. Separate the unknown threat from the usable information needed to navigate and make decisions. For each encounter specify the warning, interpretation, choice, possible consequence, escape or coping route, and recovery period. Suspense needs something the player can anticipate; unreadable controls merely obscure action.

Map a chapter's quiet investigation, ambiguous signs, commitment points, active danger, and decompression. Give quiet content a purpose: understanding the setting, reconsidering a prior event, preparing, or choosing the next risk. A scare budget should account for attention and habituation, not only event frequency. Persistent resource pressure can make relief incomplete without requiring constant pursuit.

Prototype a short sequence before building a full scare library. For dynamic events, define eligibility, repetition limits, competing cues, and recovery rules so combinations remain navigable. For authored events, test approach directions and player delays. Preserve the difference between intended uncertainty and a threat that becomes harmless when its limitations are discovered.

## Applicability and exceptions

Frictional's examples favor vulnerability and narrative attention; action-horror may deliberately reward mastery as well. Multiplayer conversation, repeated attempts, and speedrunning can change the emotional goal. Darkness and ambiguity can undermine accessibility or fairness. No fixed amount of fear, number of scares, or universal tension curve is established by these sources.

## Checks

Proposed checks: observe first and repeat attempts; record anticipated danger, actual response, avoidance, and moments of confusion. Try obvious safe-position exploits. Inspect whether resource failure permits meaningful decisions. Review randomized combinations and quiet intervals as complete sequences. Treat fear reports and completion behavior as separate observations; no such sessions ran in this research.

## Evidence

1. [Grip, 9 Years, 9 Lessons on Horror](https://frictionalgames.com/2019-10-9-years-9-lessons-on-horror/), 2019-10-15: lessons 2–9, especially attention, vagueness, role, and agency; developer article text inspected.
2. [Motive, Inside Dead Space #4: The Intensity Director](https://www.ea.com/ea-studios/motive/news/inside-dead-space-4-the-intensity-director): “Building a Stress Engine” and “No One Is Safe”; full developer account inspected. Describes the 2023 remake, not a universally validated director algorithm.

id: K-GAME-IDLE-INCREMENTAL
title: Model incremental growth and prestige around player decisions
summary: For idle and incremental games, compare production, purchase costs, multipliers and reset gains over time before choosing progression pacing. State which decisions remain meaningful during active play, waiting, offline progress and repeat runs.
kind: reference
scope: Idle and incremental resource-growth games; historical mathematical examples, not current balance values or a mandatory monetization model
keywords: [idle, incremental, clicker, prestige, growth-curve, generator, offline, automation, 방치형, 증분형, 클리커, 환생, 성장곡선, 자동화, 오프라인]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Original Kongregate articles and relevant GDC slides inspected
  application_status: Proposed planning checks; no progression simulation or gameplay trial performed
---

# Model incremental growth and prestige around player decisions

## Guidance

Source observations: Pecorella's Part I contrasts purchase-cost growth with resource production and examines generator relevance [S1]. Part III distinguishes prestige based on lifetime, maximum, or current-run earnings and cautions against treating a simplified model as accurate player behavior [S2]. The accompanying slides discuss alternate growth structures and the intended shape of repeat cycles [S3].

Proposed method: define what is accumulated, the production unit, purchase rule, unlocks, multipliers, storage limits and what resets or persists. Choose the intended decisions: purchase order, allocation, timing, collection, or discovering a new system. Model time to the next consequential change rather than only displaying large numbers.

Compare plausible purchase and reset policies across early, middle and late progression. Include discrete production cycles, offline earning, caps and automation where present. Trace one reset into the next run: what becomes faster, what new opportunity appears, and what meaningful action remains? Treat return cadence and waiting tolerance as design hypotheses to investigate with the intended audience.

## Applicability and exceptions

The cited examples describe historical versions, not current game parameters. Exponential costs and prestige are options, not requirements for every incremental game. A relaxed toy may value expression or observation more than optimization. Do not prescribe fixed reset percentages, mandatory waiting, energy gates, ads, paid acceleration, or return schedules from another game's example. Keep active effort and elapsed time distinct.

## Checks

Proposed checks: compare resource/time units, bulk purchases, milestone discontinuities, reset gains and offline limits. Look for a dominant purchase that removes intended choices, dead periods without the intended payoff, and resets that fail to change the experience. A mathematically efficient policy need not represent actual players. Record the model's assumptions and verify interpretation and pacing in authorized tests.

## Evidence

- [S1] Anthony Pecorella, [The Math of Idle Games, Part I](https://www.kongregate.com/en/pages/the-math-of-idle-games-part-i), terminology, production/cost formulas and generator comparisons. Original author article opened.
- [S2] Pecorella, [Part III](https://www.kongregate.com/en/pages/the-math-of-idle-games-part-iii), prestige statistics, model limitations and concluding advice. Original author article opened.
- [S3] Pecorella, [Quest for Progress: The Math and Design of Idle Games](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdceurope2016/presentations/Pecorella_Anthony_Quest%20for%20Progress.pdf), 2016, PDF pp. 23, 26-27, 46-53; text inspected and relevant slide screenshots requested. The full video was not viewed.
- Checked 2026-11-07. No numerical example here is a recommendation for a new game's balance; the workflow is a proposed transferable synthesis.

===== knowledge/game/K-GAME-METROIDVANIA.md =====

---
id: K-GAME-METROIDVANIA
title: Plan ability-gated exploration through readable obstacles and changed return routes
summary: For interconnected action-adventures, connect acquired abilities to recognizable barriers, useful revisits, and manageable navigation memory. Prototype traversal and route topology together so a new power changes both local action and the player's options in the wider world.
kind: reference
scope: Game planning; interconnected action-adventures with abilities and revisitation; not a universal definition of every game tagged metroidvania
keywords: [metroidvania, ability-gating, backtracking, traversal, world-map, 메트로배니아, 능력해금, 재방문, 이동, 월드설계]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Nintendo exploration guidance including developer statements and Team Cherry official game description inspected
  application_status: Reference research only; topology and movement prototype checks unexecuted
---

# Abilities, barriers, and meaningful returns

## Guidance

**Source-supported:** Nintendo's Metroid Dread report explains revisiting earlier areas with new abilities, highlighting matching map obstacles, consulting a mission log, and noticing hints near hidden progression blocks. Developer statements explicitly allow getting lost while retaining clues and encourage alternate routes on later runs [1]. Team Cherry's Hollow Knight description pairs an interconnected world with new abilities, combat, traps, distinctive areas, and optional discoveries [2]. These describe particular games; neither supplies a universal map formula.

**Reusable planning synthesis:** Draw an area graph with entrances, exits, one-way transitions, checkpoints, shortcuts, and barriers. Label each barrier by the ability, knowledge, item, or skill it actually requires; do not collapse these into one lock type. For each new ability, identify its first safe use, demanding use, interactions with existing actions, and at least one earlier place whose possibilities change.

Plan what players can recognize before they can pass. Distinct obstacles, landmarks, and map records can turn a blocked path into a remembered possibility. Decide which routes form the intended progression, which support optional rewards, and which permit deliberate sequence breaks. Document failure recovery and avoid a route that traps a player after consuming a required resource or entering without a return action.

Prototype movement dimensions and recovery before final room art, then test a small interconnected region through acquisition and revisitation. Measure travel and repeated encounter costs in context: backtracking can express mastery, expose changed meaning, or reveal a shortcut; it can also become compulsory repetition. Spread discoveries and challenge so the return trip has a reason beyond confirming that an old door now opens.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful when abilities reshape access through a persistent world. Some exploration games prioritize knowledge or environmental change instead; consult the separate exploration entry. Open routing need not mean all orders are valid. Players who enjoy being lost still need enough evidence to form a plan. Map assistance trades memory burden against discovery; neither total opacity nor complete direction is required by the sources.

## Checks

Proposed checks: traverse the graph with actual ability inventories; inspect dead ends, one-way transitions, and recovery. Observe whether players remember a usable barrier after acquiring a power. Compare first visits with return journeys and test intended alternate routes. Recheck dimensions after movement changes. No map, build, or gameplay session was tested here.

## Evidence

1. [Nintendo, Metroid Dread Report Vol. 11](https://www.nintendo.com/en-gb/News/2022/January/Metroid-Dread-Report-Vol-11-Power-through-with-these-pointers-for-exploration-and-combat-2102114.html), 2022-01-11: “Finding your bearings” and adjacent “Transmission from the dev team,” “Exploring to the fullest,” and ability guidance; text inspected, embedded videos excluded.
2. [Team Cherry, Hollow Knight](https://www.hollowknight.com/): “Use Your Skills and Reflexes to Survive” and “Evocative Hand-Drawn Art”; official game description inspected. Marketing description supports stated features, not undocumented production methods or playtest outcomes.

kind: reference
scope: Competitive real-time multiplayer; clarity and network-sensitive design
keywords: [multiplayer, PvP, fairness, netcode, latency, server authority, client-side prediction, server correction, server rewind, clarity, VFX, competitive integrity, prototype, 멀티플레이, 대전, 공정성, 지연, 서버 권한, 클라이언트 예측, 가독성, 판정, 기획]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-19'
context:
  evidence_check: Riot official design and engineering article sections read
  application_status: Proposed design checks; no multiplayer simulation or user study run
---
# Define multiplayer fairness through information and resolution contracts

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Riot's clarity article ties ability attention to gameplay importance, discusses effect/hitbox alignment, and seeks clarity parity across skins. VALORANT's developers describe competing goals for responsiveness, fairness, territory holding, and broad hardware/network support. Their server-authoritative approach combines prediction, correction, and bounded historical hit resolution. These describe Riot's goals and implementation, not a guarantee that every match is fair.

**Transferable synthesis.** Write two contracts for each contested action: what each participant can know, and which event determines the result. Trace perceive opponent → commit action → resolve contention → receive feedback → adapt. A clear effect with surprising authoritative timing still breaks understanding; accurate resolution with an obscured cue still denies usable information.

With **server authority**, the server decides the accepted simulation state. **Client-side prediction** lets the local client display the expected result of its own input before confirmation. A **server correction** brings a diverging client back to the accepted state. **Server rewind** addresses a different delay. For hit registration, Riot describes testing the shot against historical positions corresponding to what the shooter could see, subject to a limit. When players report a hit after taking cover, compare the shooter's view, the target's view and the server's evaluation time. The rewind limit is a design choice with consequences for both players.

Before adding an ability, cosmetic, camera effect, or movement burst, inspect team recognition, threat priority, collision representation, and latency interaction together. Decide the intended advantage of holding, chasing, initiating, or escaping. A networking model cannot choose those design priorities for the team. Give designers a condition matrix so the same interaction is reviewed under local, delayed, jittery, and asymmetric connections.

Classify a complaint before changing balance: ambiguous visual information, simulation disagreement, unavailable counterplay, or numerical dominance require different experiments. Review the loser's and winner's views together; one convincing local recording cannot establish the entire event sequence.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for shooters, MOBAs, fighting games, and other contested real-time systems. Different genres may choose rollback, lockstep, or other authority policies; the cited model is not universal. Turn-based games still need information and resolution contracts but have different delay sensitivities. This entry does not cover matchmaking, social moderation, ranked population health, or economy fairness.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Test identical abilities with alternate cosmetics in crowded combat. Ask players to identify threat, direction, and affected area. Replay a contested interaction from both clients and an authoritative event log under emulated delay, jitter, and loss. Inspect corrections and feedback after cover transitions. Define acceptable discrepancies for the intended audience before interpreting results; report observations separately from perceived fairness.

## Evidence

- [Riot, Clarity in League](https://www.leagueoflegends.com/en-us/news/dev/clarity-in-league/), “Abilities” and skin “Abilities”/clarity-parity discussion; official text opened 2026-11-07. Examples describe author intent; no skin comparison performed.
- [Riot, Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode), “A Few VALORANT Design Goals,” “Resolving Disagreements” and “Confirming Hit Registration.” Official text first checked 2026-11-07. The movement and combat mechanisms were rechecked 2026-11-19. This is a historical 2020 explanation. Current implementation and service targets remain unverified.

keywords: [narrative-adventure, interactive-fiction, branching, state, choice, ink, 내러티브어드벤처, 인터랙티브픽션, 분기, 선택, 상태관리]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Official ink tutorial and inkle development article inspected
  application_status: Reference research only; no script compiled or story playtested
---

# Choices, state, and narrative traversal

## Guidance

**Source-supported:** inkle's tutorial demonstrates linked passages, choices, visit-dependent content, repeatable versus once-only choices, live preview, and warnings for unintended loose ends [1]. Its 80 Days development account describes complexity from interconnected travel, resources, timing, and special cases. For balancing, the team used the original novel's approximately eighty-day itinerary as a reference route and compared other journeys against that spine [2]. These are implementation capabilities and a specific production example, not proof that every branching story needs the same structure.

**Reusable planning synthesis:** Define the player's role and recurring decision: whom to trust, where to travel, what to investigate, or what to spend. For each choice record the information visible beforehand, intended commitment, state changed, immediate response, and later content that reads that state. Distinguish expressive choices from strategic ones so the presentation does not promise an unimplemented consequence.

Sketch a passage-and-state graph before writing every variant. Use shared destinations with conditional acknowledgments when a permanent branch would add cost without an intended experiential difference. Preserve consequences that matter after reconnection. Plan content units around actions and discoveries, including how a player obtains necessary information; lore volume alone does not create agency.

Prototype one representative journey including a consequential choice, reconnection, setback, and ending. Estimate reading and decision time alongside travel, currency, or relationship pressures. A reference itinerary can reveal unreasonable costs without proving that all paths are balanced. Reserve production time for rare state combinations, unavailable choices, recovery situations, and localization.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful when authored text and persistent state shape progression. Environmental storytelling and cinematic action need separate navigation and encounter methods. Converging branches can preserve expression but weaken strategic consequences if nothing remembers the choice. Unlimited branching increases writing, translation, and verification work. An eighty-day benchmark is specific to 80 Days, not a universal pacing target.

## Checks

Proposed checks: inspect choice labels against the resulting action; trace each knowledge-dependent response to a prior source; traverse representative state combinations and all authored endings; inspect loops and resource exhaustion for intended recovery or failure. Ask playtesters what they believed a choice would change, then compare with actual behavior. No reader or gameplay results were generated here.

## Evidence

1. [inkle, Writing web-based interactive fiction with ink](https://www.inklestudios.com/ink/web-tutorial/): “The anatomy of an ink story,” “Conditional content,” and “Advanced ink”; official tutorial text inspected, linked manual not assumed inspected.
2. [inkle, Journey's End...](https://www.inklestudios.com/2014/07/14/approaching-journeys-end), 2014-07-14: “The world, turns out, is big” and “Balancing”; full developer article inspected. Historical production account, not measured evidence for a new game.

scope: Platformer movement and level prototyping; tile-based 2D examples with limited 3D transfer
keywords: [platformer, movement, collision, moving platform, axis-aligned bounding box, AABB, level design, animation, prototype, 플랫포머, 이동, 충돌, 축 정렬 경계 상자, 발판, 레벨 설계, 기획, 프로토타입]
status: active

context:
  evidence_check: Author physics article and Nintendo interview read; Celeste GDC abstract only
  application_status: Proposed movement and level checks; no controller or level tested
---
# Establish platformer movement contracts before composing challenges

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Maddy Thorson's physics article distinguishes actors, solids, carrying, pushing, and context-dependent crushing behavior; pushing has priority over carrying. Nintendo's Wonder developers describe animation as state communication and explain balancing facial expressiveness with readable travel direction. The Celeste GDC abstract identifies stage creation, area arrangement, and story integration as its topics; it does not substantiate particular teaching sequences.

**Transferable synthesis.** Define the movement contract before content production: what counts as grounded, what moving surfaces carry, what pushes, and what happens when space runs out. Write player intent → input → movement → contact → feedback → retry as the local loop. A geometry change can alter contact timing, camera visibility, hazard readability, and checkpoint cost together.

An **axis-aligned bounding box (AABB)** represents a 2D collider by a rectangle whose sides stay parallel to the world axes. Thorson's implementation uses integer collider coordinates, accumulates fractional motion and tests each whole-pixel step along one axis. In that model, check a moving platform that both supports and pushes the same rider. The push clears the overlap and the rider receives only that displacement on the axis. Test angled or rotating surfaces separately when the target movement model includes them.

Prototype representative interactions rather than only a pleasant jump on flat ground. Include side contacts, reversals, ceilings, moving surfaces, and competing contacts. Once these outcomes are predictable, compose rooms around one readable decision, then add combinations that test the same movement vocabulary. This room progression is a proposed synthesis, not a claim about the unviewed Celeste presentation.

Choose animation, camera lead, sound, and landing feedback to expose the movement state without hiding the next action. Reserve optional routes for additional mastery only when the main route still communicates its own requirements. Revisit the movement contract whenever a new ability changes reach or contact rules.

## Applicability and exceptions

Most directly applicable to precise 2D movement. The cited integer/AABB implementation is a specific solution, not a requirement for slope physics, rotation, continuous collision, or 3D traversal. Deliberately slippery, physical, or chaotic movement can be a goal; consistency should then mean understandable system behavior rather than instant control. Accessibility options may change execution demand without changing route logic.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Maintain a tiny interaction course with standing, clinging, pushing, and crushing cases. Replay it after controller changes. Ask new players to predict landing and platform outcomes, then compare prediction with result. Test room comprehension separately from completion, and inspect retry distance, camera framing, and optional-route visibility after each geometry change.

## Evidence

- [Thorson, Celeste & TowerFall Physics](https://www.mattmakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_towerfall_physics/index.html), opening constraints, “Actor Basics,” “Moving Solids Prep” and “The Heavy Lifting.” Author text first checked 2026-11-07. Collider assumptions, axis stepping and push/carry priority rechecked 2026-11-19. No code executed.
- [Nintendo, Wonder Chapter 2](https://www.nintendo.com/au/news-and-articles/ask-the-developer-vol-11-super-mario-bros-wonder-chapter-2/), state-animation and travel-direction discussion; official interview text opened 2026-11-07. Embedded footage not analyzed.
- [GDC, Designing Celeste](https://gdcvault.com/play/1024307/Level-Design-Workshop-Designing-Celeste), “Overview”; opened 2026-11-07. Abstract only; full talk not viewed.

id: "K-GAME-PROTOTYPE-PLAYTEST"
title: "Match a prototype and playtest to the design uncertainty"
summary: "Before expanding a game design, select the smallest playable representation that can challenge a consequential assumption. Separate observed player behavior, interpretation, proposed fixes, and evidence that a revised build actually improves the result."
kind: "reference"
scope: "Game design prototyping, usability and experience research across genres; not a fixed schedule, sample size or automatic execution authorization"
keywords: ["prototype", "playtest", "hypothesis", "usability", "iteration", "vertical-slice", "telemetry", "프로토타입", "플레이테스트", "가설", "시제품", "검증", "재미"]
status: "active"
checked_at: "2026-11-07"
context: {"evidence_check": "Named primary source text or slides inspected; source-specific access limits in Evidence", "application_status": "Source-based reference and proposed synthesis; no project gameplay trial"}
---

# Match a prototype and playtest to the design uncertainty

## Guidance

Source observations: Experimental Gameplay Project authors describe starting from a manipulable core mechanic and keeping exploratory builds small [S1]. Valve developer Ken Birdwell describes observing outside players without hints and feeding findings back into design [S2]. Games researcher Graham McAllister recommends aligning on prioritized questions that upcoming observations can answer [S3].

Proposed method: state the uncertain claim and the decision affected by its failure. Choose a paper model, greybox, interaction prototype, or representative integrated slice according to that claim. Include the feedback essential to the question; mark omitted systems and temporary assumptions.

Prepare tasks without teaching the answer. Observe actions and stumbling points, then ask what players understood. Keep unfamiliar and practiced players distinguishable. Capture build/version and conditions. Convert a finding into the smallest supported change, rerun the affected observation on the changed build, and retain contrary evidence.

## Applicability and exceptions

The seven-day project and Valve sessions are historical cases, not universal budgets or sample sizes. A toy can investigate feel without proving a complete game. A paper model can test choices while omitting execution timing; a polished slice can hide content-production cost. Separate usability, bug finding, balancing, experience, and performance questions. Recruitment, builds, data collection, and deployment still require the actual task scope.

## Checks

Proposed checks: could the chosen task expose a broken assumption? Is an observer hint masking the problem? Does telemetry identify behavior while leaving its cause uncertain? Has a proposed fix been retested rather than merely documented? Report sample and representation limits; a positive small test does not establish retention or population-wide preference.

## Evidence

- [S1] Experimental Gameplay Project authors, [How to Prototype a Game in Under 7 Days](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/game-platforms/how-to-prototype-a-game-in-under-7-days), 2005; Development / Build the Toy First. Original authored article opened.
- [S2] Ken Birdwell, [The Cabal](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/the-cabal-valve-s-design-process-for-creating-i-half-life-i-), 1999; Pearls Before Swine, playtesting and subsequent design revision. Original developer article opened.
- [S3] Graham McAllister, [Aligning on a Clear Purpose](https://grahammcallister.com/playtesting-book/step-1-alignment-on-clear-purpose), opening hypotheses and Step 1 Outcome. Author page opened.
- Sources checked 2026-11-07. Proposed checks were not exercised on a real game in this task.

kind: reference
scope: Game planning; authored systemic logic and environmental puzzles; not a complete method for trivia, hidden-object, or procedural puzzle economies
keywords: [puzzle, deduction, rule-discovery, level-design, playtesting, 퍼즐, 추론, 규칙발견, 레벨설계, 플레이테스트]
status: active

context:
  evidence_check: Developer articles opened; embedded talk not inspected; source claims separated from planning synthesis
  application_status: Reference research only; proposed checks not run on a game
---

# Puzzle discovery and content structure

## Guidance

**Source-supported:** Patrick Traynor's strategy collection suggests requiring a particular interaction or deduction, exploring mechanic pairs and unusual geometry, and using playtester misunderstandings or alternate solutions to generate levels. He describes iteration as producing many discarded drafts and potentially several useful variants [1]. Jonathan Blow's developer post describes deleting an unsatisfactory area containing roughly 25 puzzles; it also cautions that puzzle counts are not directly comparable when their scale differs [2]. His talk announcement frames design as exploring a system's consequences and seeking clear expression of its ideas [3].

**Reusable planning synthesis:** First define the player's initial rule model, visible goal, legal actions, and the new inference a level makes possible. Build a minimal playable arrangement that requires that inference; separately record intended solutions, alternatives, and accidental bypasses. An alternate solution may expose another worthwhile concept rather than simply being a defect.

Organize content by knowledge prerequisites: introduce an interaction, vary its context, combine it with another, then revisit it under a changed assumption. This is a candidate sequence, not a requirement to make every puzzle teach exactly one fact. Give players room to experiment and decide when to leave an optional challenge. Plan undo, reset, and feedback according to the cost of experimentation. Difficulty can come from unfamiliar reasoning, execution, memory, or search; identify which you actually intend before adding complexity.

## Applicability and exceptions

Best suited to finite authored systems whose rules can be explored. Timing puzzles, collaborative puzzles, riddles, and culturally dependent clues need additional evidence. Minimal presentation can aid focus but may remove orientation or accessibility cues. More possible states do not establish more valuable discoveries. The cited deletion is one production decision, not an optimal rejection rate.

## Checks

Proposed checks: observe a fresh player without supplying the missing rule; record what they noticed, inferred, and tried. Compare failures with information actually available. Replay after changes to confirm solvability and inspect unintended shortcuts. Distinguish productive experimentation from repeated guesses caused by unreadable feedback. These checks remain unexecuted here.

## Evidence

1. [Traynor, Puzzle Level Idea Strategies](https://cwpat.me/misc/puzzle-level-idea-strategies/), 2022-07-14: headings “Find an interaction and force it,” “Clarify a misunderstanding,” “Turn alternate solutions,” and “Other notes”; full article inspected.
2. [Blow, How many puzzles are in The Witness?](https://www.the-witness.net/news/2011/02/how-many-puzzles-are-in-the-witness/), 2011-02-08: opening paragraphs before comments; developer text inspected, reader comments excluded.
3. [Designing to Reveal the Nature of the Universe](https://the-witness.net/news/2011/11/designing-to-reveal-the-nature-of-the-universe/), 2011-11-02: author introduction/abstract only; embedded talk and comments are outside the inspected evidence.

scope: Circuit and time-trial racing; Forza Motorsport and Trackmania developer cases
keywords: [racing, handling, corner scoring, track design, telemetry, consistency, competition, prototype, 레이싱, 조작감, 코너, 트랙 설계, 기록, 일관성, 기획]
status: active

  evidence_check: Official Forza design post and Ubisoft Nadeo interface post read

# Make racing improvement legible across handling and competition

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Forza Motorsport director Chris Esaki describes learning through corner scoring, car building, and competition; he also reports rebuilding tracks because a new tire/suspension model interacted poorly with old surfaces. Ubisoft Nadeo explains adding personal bests, crash frequency, first-place counts, and live respawn notifications to help spectators understand competitive performance. These are design intentions and development accounts, not measured learning gains.

**Transferable synthesis.** Define the repeated driving loop as anticipate corner → choose line and speed → control traction → observe result → revise next attempt. Decide which feedback explains a choice, rather than only displaying a final rank. Sector deltas and replay comparison can locate a loss, while contextual signals can distinguish overdriving, a collision, or a strategic concession.

Treat handling, track geometry, camera, sound, assists, and progression as coupled systems. When grip, suspension, braking, or steering response changes, revisit curbs, jumps, sightlines, braking markers, and recovery space. A physically plausible model still needs tracks and feedback suited to the intended controller and audience.

Separate pace from consistency when designing time trials, races, and spectator tools. Rewarding a single fast lap creates a different risk strategy from rewarding reliable finishes. If upgrades or assists change performance, decide how comparisons remain interpretable and how players learn the cause of improvement. The proposed loop and comparison policy are synthesis, not a claim that either cited game implements every suggestion.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for circuit racing, time attack, and repeatable driving challenges. Arcade drifting, combat racing, rally navigation, and open-world exploration need different success signals and recovery assumptions. Spectator telemetry is evidence for communication design, not proof that it improves driving skill. The 2023 Forza launch account does not establish current progression rules.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Use a short test circuit with a braking zone, linked corners, curb, and recovery area. Compare controller types and assist settings with a stable car. Ask players to predict where time was lost before showing telemetry. Re-run representative tracks after physics changes. Inspect whether rewards favor the intended risk behavior, and whether observers can explain position changes without seeing every car.

## Evidence

- [Esaki, Competitive Ground for Building Skill](https://forza.net/news/forza-motorsport-preview/), “Building Skill Through Competition,” especially corner scoring and the track-rebuild paragraph; official 2023 article opened 2026-11-07. Embedded gameplay not viewed; current systems not audited.
- [Ubisoft Nadeo, World Championships UI](https://blog.trackmania.com/2023/09/04/a-new-and-fresh-user-interface-for-the-upcoming-world-championships/), “Enhanced statistics” and “Live feed”; official text opened 2026-11-07. Spectator-facing rationale only; no viewer study inspected.

status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-11'
context:
  authority: The working game's current design, code and acceptance conditions govern adoption.
  evidence_check: Historical retrieval and runtime examples with limited engine and workload scope. Each linked entry identifies its own evidence.
---

# Retrieve applicable evidence throughout game development

Registered source references identify historical evidence. Their presence does not establish current access or a new execution check.

## Guidance

Before game planning, architecture, implementation, debugging or optimization, identify the concrete decision, feature or observed symptom. Record relevant project/revision, engine/package versions, platform/input, intended experience, frame/resource budget, and network/state authority. For defects, preserve the reproduction and measured symptom. Unknown values remain gaps; retrieve useful evidence while resolving them.

Search the [game catalog](index.md) through [knowledge retrieval](../../skills/vegabond-knowledge/references/retrieval.md), including relevant code knowledge and problem records. Start with one to three useful details and expand for a concrete dependency, conflict or unanswered question. Use symptom and mechanism variants when a query misses; consult the [technique guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md) as a fallback map. A top-ranked hit is a candidate, not an adoption decision. Do not read the complete collection on every task.

Use the [experience framework](K-GAME-DESIGN-FRAMEWORK.md) for concept and hybrid questions, [prototype/playtest](K-GAME-PROTOTYPE-PLAYTEST.md) for uncertain experience claims, [economy](K-GAME-ECONOMY.md) for resource interactions, and [accessibility/onboarding](K-GAME-ACCESSIBILITY-ONBOARDING.md) for interaction barriers. Select genre references by what is being designed, not only a marketing label. A cooperative roguelike may require action, cooperative pacing, run structure and resource methods at different steps.

When a query returns nothing, distinguish an unfamiliar expression from a missing required contract. Try mechanism terms, another language or a narrowly broader scope once, and use the guide for a relevant known mechanism. If an exact vendor API, private format or version guarantee still lacks evidence, name the missing contract and the dependent commitment; request the smallest authoritative document or experiment needed. A generic related hit cannot establish that exact guarantee. Complete independent, source-supported analysis while leaving that commitment conditional; no match is not proof of nonexistence.

When several techniques match, split the request into decisions before choosing. Compare the representation, required inputs, guarantee, authority, version and measured cost each candidate addresses. Different layers can need different techniques: route search does not resolve local avoidance, visible surface detail does not create collision, and visual quality does not establish competitive visibility. Adopt a combination only where each part has a role and compatible conditions; reject or defer mismatched candidates. Do not decide by rank, repeated citations or the number of matching terms.

Read candidates' Guidance, applicability, checks and Evidence. Compare the actual engine/version, data ownership, target hardware and gameplay constraints; a historical source-game implementation is not a current engine API guarantee. Mark each consequential proposal **adopt**, **conditional** or **reject**, with its reason. Inspect original sources or research a missing detail before dependent work when the summary cannot support it. Distinguish source observations, transferable synthesis and untested local assumptions.

Keep a compact application trace in the existing work brief or design: **problem/constraints → entry ID and useful section/source locator → decision and reason → intended change → check and observed result/gap**. A source citation alone is insufficient. For example, a late-jump complaint can use CONTROLLER's buffered-input window while retaining TIMESTEP's simulation contract; an expensive forest needs measured geometry/pixel/shadow costs before choosing FOLIAGE's instancing or LOD. Tie the chosen method to a small representative prototype or regression/performance check; do not report a proposed check as passed.

After implementation or investigation, return the actual outcome, rejected analogy, failed condition and useful correction to [knowledge](../../skills/vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md). Update an existing shared entry when the evidence changes its applicability. Keep project-specific incidents, data, code, logs and tuning values with the originating project. Only demonstrated local results become local lessons or verified safeguards. Revisit retrieval when reproduction, platform, authority or design intent changes; preserve still-valid answers.

For large plans, connect retrieved methods to the existing hierarchy, dependent systems and acceptance checks using [large-plan coordination](../../skills/vegabond-planning/references/large-plans.md). Keep the project's own design and playtest evidence authoritative. Refresh affected retrieval after a change; preserve still-valid information rather than rereading the complete collection.

## Applicability and exceptions

This is the user's requested retrieval convention for game work. It does not require techniques in unrelated software or authorize changing other projects. A simple established local pattern can reuse a valid prior lookup and state why it still applies. Hybrid genres may contain conflicting recommendations: relate the conflict to the intended experience and test the relevant tradeoff. ARPG loot references do not cover all role-playing, industrial-management references do not cover all simulation, and one 4X example does not establish every strategy design.

For a purely mechanical spelling or formatting proposal whose supplied local input fully settles the change and preserved fields, use that input as the governing evidence. Do not initiate game-technique searches solely because the file belongs to a game. Search only if an actual contract, dependency or uncertainty could change the requested action; verify the exact diff and preserve unrelated fields.

The collection is a broad starting corpus, not an exhaustive or permanently current genre encyclopedia. Gaps such as a specific sport, fighting-game format, VR embodiment, MMO social structure or platform policy require focused research when relevant. Store entries with inspected evidence and explicit limits; a search snippet, unavailable video, or untested suggestion cannot become a verified method by repetition. Use this store in another project only within its configured or authorized source scope.

## Checks

- Does the retrieved entry match the actual decision or reproduced symptom, engine/version, hardware, authority, genre mechanics and production conditions?
- Does the brief contain an action or check informed by the evidence, rather than only a citation?
- Is adoption, conditional use or rejection explicit, with source-case results separated from new-game hypotheses and pending real tests?
- Does a rule conflict with project authority, intended ambiguity, accessibility, competitive fairness or another system?
- Is a missing source or coverage gap visible, with only dependent decisions left unresolved?

Catalog/schema validation establishes storage and discoverability, not gameplay quality, balance, usability or market success.

## Evidence

- The 2026-11-07 request to collect and reuse game planning and implementation references established this convention. The [knowledge procedure](../../skills/vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md), [scout](../../skills/vegabond-scout/SKILL.md), [planning](../../skills/vegabond-planning/SKILL.md), [code](../../skills/vegabond-code/SKILL.md) and [code design](../../skills/vegabond-code-design/SKILL.md) provide the corresponding retrieval and execution workflow.
- Historical Unity 6000.0.77f1 and Unreal Engine 5.8.3 experiments illustrate why the question determines the evidence. A route may fit the agent and still leave agents blocking one another. Look up [pathfinding](K-GAME-TECH-PATH.md) for route validity and [crowd movement](K-GAME-TECH-CROWD.md) for collision, completion and waiting. Where arrivals continue, compare every offered request, including rejections and unfinished work. The observations cover particular geometries, arrival patterns and movement rules. [Dynamic routes](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-dynamic-results-md) · [Opposing traffic](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-traffic-results-md) · [Sustained arrivals](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-stream-results-md) · [Admission policies](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-admission-results-md)

status: active

context:
  evidence_check: Harmonix developer history and official guide plus osu official wiki page read
  application_status: Proposed genre transfer; no chart or hardware timing tested
---
# Separate rhythmic challenge from chart readability and device delay

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Harmonix developer James Fleming describes separately addressing audio/video alignment and the delay through the display/controller loop for scoring. Its calibration guide offers automatic and manual routes followed by a song test. The official osu! wiki presents proportional time/spacing as a readability technique, especially for easier charts, while acknowledging deliberate spacing variation at higher difficulty. The wiki is community-maintained official documentation, not a controlled study.

**Transferable synthesis.** Define listen/read cue → predict beat → execute input → receive judgment → recover as the repeated loop. Decide which musical layer the chart represents and make repeated motifs recognizable. Increase difficulty by choosing the intended burden: rhythmic inference, reading density, movement, coordination, or endurance. Raising all of them together makes a failed attempt harder to diagnose.

Treat chart timing, visual travel, input capture, sound output, and judgment feedback as a chain. Widening a hit window can hide device offset while weakening the intended scoring distinction. Conversely, perfect authored note times do not guarantee that the player sees and hears aligned cues. Provide a calibration path and a simple reference passage before interpreting difficult-chart failures.

Use consistent chart relationships as a teaching vocabulary, then vary them deliberately for musical emphasis or advanced reading. This is a proposed transfer across games, not a requirement that lane games or one-button games copy osu!'s spatial technique. Link difficulty selection and practice tools to the skill being trained rather than only a single aggregate label.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for authored music games and rhythm-driven actions. Free-tempo music, improvisation, expressive timing, and deliberately deceptive cues need explicit timing models and different consistency expectations. Calibration values depend on the hardware path; historical Rock Band timings and example display settings are not defaults for new systems. This entry does not establish accessible play without visual or audio channels.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Compare a sparse reference pattern and a demanding chart on the same setup. Record early/late distributions separately from wrong-input errors. Change audio route and display mode, then recalibrate and repeat. Ask players which musical element and cue they followed. Test repeated motifs for accidental notation changes, and check whether a miss allows recovery before the next phrase.

## Evidence

- [Fleming, Rock Band Calibration](https://www.harmonixmusic.com/blog/rock-band-10th-anniversary-blogs-james-fleming), “Part 1, the Fight” and “Part 2, the Makening”; official developer text opened 2026-11-07. Historical engineering account, no hardware reproduction.
- [Harmonix Calibration Guide](https://www.harmonixmusic.com/how-to-calibration/), “Auto Calibration” and “Manual Calibration”; official text opened 2026-11-07. Product-specific instructions; example numbers not reused.
- [osu!, Time-distance equality](https://osu.ppy.sh/wiki/en/Beatmapping/Mapping_techniques/Time-distance_equality), “Usage” and “Benefits”; official wiki text opened 2026-11-07. Technique guidance, not universal chart law.

status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Actual GDC slide text and Mega Crit original newsletter opened; no talk transcript used
  application_status: Proposed content and balance workflow; no card pool, telemetry, or run playtest executed
---

# Evaluate deckbuilder choices in run context and control content scope

## Guidance

**Source observations.** Giovannetti's slides aim for useful contexts for all cards while avoiding options that distort the whole game. They pair iteration and feedback with metrics, warn against treating data as a conclusion, and identify Ascension as skill stratification. Casey Yano's production account explains how visual limitations helped narrow narrative scope around deckbuilding and the core loop.

**Proposed reusable method.** Map a run as encounter information → tactical spending → reward selection → route or recovery choice → next encounter. Record both immediate survival and future deck consequences. For each card, define its intended situation, opportunity cost, enabling pieces, and a situation where declining it is reasonable. Inspect acquisition probability and encounter demands together: a coherent combo that rarely assembles may function as a trap rather than a reliable strategic branch.

Instrument offers, selections, skips, deck state, relevant upgrades, encounter, difficulty, and outcome. Compare cards within matching contexts before changing them. Use replay observation or tester explanation to decide whether a surprising metric reflects power, misunderstanding, selection bias, or experienced players rescuing difficult runs. Change one clear hypothesis and retain the previous build for comparison.

Treat card, relic, enemy, event, and route additions as interaction costs. Prototype a small complete run with several distinct decisions before multiplying content. Keep a cut criterion for mechanics that require substantial explanation, bespoke presentation, or exceptions without enriching the intended loop.

## Applicability and exceptions

Use for single-player runs with randomized offers and persistent consequences within a run. Competitive collectible card games require separate fairness and economy evidence; traditional dungeon roguelikes are not fully covered. Equal pick or win rates are not a universal goal. Rare, difficult, or situational cards may be valuable. Slides establish explicit developer takeaways, not the spoken reasoning behind every chart. The production anecdote supports a scope tradeoff, not a rule to remove narrative.

## Checks

- Trace several viable and failed runs; identify decisions that actually changed later options.
- Review unpopular cards in their intended contexts before buffing them; inspect dominant combinations for lost alternatives.
- Segment by difficulty and player experience, and report sample size and selection limitations.
- Estimate added interaction, text, localization, art, and testing work before approving a content batch.

## Evidence

- [Giovannetti: Metrics Driven Design and Balance](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2019/presentations/Giovannetti_Anthony_SlayTheSpire.pdf): actual 22-page GDC 2019 slides, especially PDF pp. 7, 10–16, 21. Slide text inspected; no complete video or transcript reviewed.
- [Mega Crit Neowsletter—October 2024](https://megacrit.com/news/2024-10-02-neowsletter-issue-3/): “On Aesthetics Driving Game Design,” final paragraphs on humanoid readability and narrative scope. Original developer text read; retrospective production account.

keywords: [RPG, ARPG, progression, itemization, loot, build, respec, economy, 성장, 아이템화, 전리품, 빌드, 재분배, 역할수행게임]
status: active

context:
  evidence_check: Original Blizzard article text opened; historical proposals distinguished from later changes
  application_status: Proposed planning checks; no local progression model or gameplay tested
---

# Separate build identity, loot evaluation, and upgrade investment

## Guidance

**Source observations.** Blizzard's 2020 design update paired class identity with customization and described the tension between experimenting and committing through respec costs. Its 2024 overhaul simplified dropped affixes, shifted customization into crafting, and reduced drops while adjusting salvage materials. These are different development stages, not one stable specification.

**Proposed reusable method.** Draw the loop as encounter → reward recognition → comparison → equip/salvage → investment → changed combat options. For each transition, identify the player's question, information shown, resource spent, and recovery path. Separate three budgets: time spent evaluating drops, power gained, and cost of changing direction. A reduction in drop count must therefore be checked against crafting supply as well as excitement.

Make a progression sheet for early learning, build formation, and endgame refinement. Assign each tier a new decision or capability; explain numerical increases that introduce no new behavior. Record which power comes from character choices, equipment, and their interaction. For a proposed respec restriction, state the intended commitment and the experiment it might prevent. Schedule an iteration pass before authoring the full item pool, since affix changes affect tooltips, crafting, encounter requirements, and rewards.

## Applicability and exceptions

Use for repeatable combat with build experimentation and loot investment. Narrative RPG progression, party development, MMORPG social economies, and monetization need additional evidence. Permanent character identity can justify costly changes; a short experimental run may favor easy revision. Neither source establishes an ideal respec price, drop rate, or affix count. The 2020 proposal must not be presented as current Diablo IV behavior.

## Checks

- Ask a new tester to explain an upgrade and its combat effect without external guides; record comparison time and misunderstood conditions.
- Simulate several build changes using the actual currency, salvage, and upgrade rules; inspect scarcity and irreversible mistakes.
- Test a weak but appealing build against intended encounters, then distinguish execution difficulty, unavailable items, and inadequate power.
- Review an item-pool change against reward frequency, inventory pressure, crafting input supply, and content production cost.

## Evidence

- [Diablo IV Quarterly Update—December 2020](https://news.blizzard.com/en-us/article/23583664/diablo-iv-quarterly-updatedecember-2020): opening design ideals and “Skill Tree.” Full relevant article text read; pre-release intent, not shipped-rule verification.
- [Season 4: Loot Reborn](https://news.blizzard.com/en-us/article/24077223/galvanize-your-legend-in-season-4-loot-reborn): “General Item Updates,” “Tempering,” and “Masterworking.” Original article text read; historical 2024 overhaul, not a current balance reference.

Source inspection supports the observations only. The proposed checks remain unexecuted.

---
# Tune shooter weapon roles together with visual recognition

## Guidance

**Source claims.** Jaime Griesemer's Halo 3 presentation compares sniper-rifle changes against the problems they address and their collateral effects. It distinguishes ammunition constraints from shot spacing and considers strength outside the intended role. Valve's TF2 paper explains that distinctive class silhouettes and controlled shading support identification under varied lighting. Neither source establishes a universal weapon interval or required art style.

**Transferable synthesis.** Write a weapon's job as a situation: range, exposure, target type, movement state, and available counter-action. Then map acquire target → aim → fire → confirm → recover/reposition. A parameter can affect several steps: reload changes exposure and encounter rhythm; projectile speed changes lead prediction and available evasions; ammunition affects sustained pressure and map routes.

Compare candidate fixes by what they preserve as well as what they reduce. If a precision weapon dominates close range, test the acquisition and recovery conditions rather than automatically reducing every hit's satisfaction. Pair this with perception: opponents must recognize the threat early enough for the intended response. Evaluate character shape, weapon pose, muzzle effects, sound, background detail, and lighting as one communication system.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for arena, tactical, hero, and PvE shooters, with separate assumptions for aim devices, latency, enemy AI, and team size. Equal statistics do not imply equivalent roles. Deliberate identity concealment, realism, or noisy spectacle may conflict with immediate recognition; specify the tradeoff and preserve another information channel. A historical Halo adjustment is evidence about that decision, not today's optimal value.

## Checks

**Proposed checks, not executed:** Build a small map with close, medium, and long engagements. Compare candidate changes with controlled loadouts before testing pickups, economy, and teams. Log misses, second-shot opportunities, reload deaths, and role-breaking successes. Run brief identification tests at gameplay size across backgrounds; then test moving combat where attention is divided. Ask players what they saw and could have done, separating recognition failure from aiming failure.

## Evidence

- [Griesemer, Design in Detail](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc10/slides/Griesemer_Jaime_DesignInDetail_Sniper_Halo3.pdf), “Making the Change” and “Balance by limiting strengths,” text near the final decision; official GDC-hosted developer slides and notes opened 2026-11-07. Video not viewed; case-specific timings not adopted.
- [Valve, Illustrative Rendering in TF2](https://cdn.fastly.steamstatic.com/apps/valve/2007/NPAR07_IllustrativeRenderingInTeamFortress2.pdf), abstract and §4.1 “Character Modeling,” PDF pp. 1–2; official paper text opened 2026-11-07. Design rationale checked; no current gameplay recognition study performed.
