scope: Factory automation and industrial resource management; Factorio 2.0 and Space Age design posts from 2023–2024
keywords: [simulation, management, factory, automation, production, logistics, bottleneck, recipe, 시뮬레이션, 경영, 자동화, 생산, 물류, 병목, 제작법]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Three Wube original developer posts opened and relevant sections read
  application_status: Proposed design and prototype checks; no factory simulation or player test executed
---

# Make production constraints discoverable before expanding recipe breadth

## Guidance

**Source observations.** Wube's Factoriopedia addresses both ingredient discovery and finding uses for an item. Trigger technologies connect unlocks to encountering or producing materials. Gleba's development replaced many direct-output plants with fewer plants and deeper processing; spoilage makes throughput and waste handling consequential.

**Proposed reusable method.** Draw a material-flow graph with sources, transformations, transport limits, buffers, byproducts, upkeep, and sinks. Add the information path: how does a player discover each input, output use, unlock, and failure reason? The core loop should let players identify a constraint, change the system, and observe its effect. Expose sufficient rates and traces to distinguish missing supply, transport saturation, blocked output, and unavailable technology.

Choose one constraint that changes a planning habit, such as perishable inventory or competing uses for a seed. Connect it to existing machines and transport instead of multiplying unrelated recipes. Define startup, steady operation, shortage, shutdown, and restart behavior. If waste remains physical, provide a comprehensible removal or reuse path; if it disappears, ensure the player can still diagnose the loss.

Stage unlocks around demonstrated interactions where appropriate, then introduce optimization after the player has a working chain. Budget recipe authoring, icons, tooltips, graph navigation, and failure feedback together. A compact but interacting chain is a useful prototype before adding resources or production tiers.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies to systemic industrial management where understanding and improving flows is the intended activity. It does not cover social simulation, city politics, financial management, or realistic industrial safety. Some players enjoy broad recipe catalogs; breadth is not inherently a defect. Discovery games may hide relationships deliberately, while an optimization game may need exact rates. Trigger unlocks can constrain player freedom and must fit the intended learning contract. Historical developer posts establish design rationale, not current numerical rules.

## Checks

- Give testers a stalled chain and ask them to locate the cause using only in-game information.
- Calculate material and energy conservation under normal production, overflow, starvation, and restart; inspect waste accumulation and deadlocks.
- Compare a new recipe's decision with existing ones; identify changes in layout, scheduling, allocation, or transport.
- Measure time spent diagnosing, redesigning, waiting, and repeating manual work; use player explanations to interpret those times.

## Evidence

- [FFF #397: Factoriopedia](https://www.factorio.com/blog/post/fff-397): “The core of the problem” and “The Factoriopedia.” Original text read; UI rationale, not usability-test results.
- [FFF #376: Research and Technology](https://www.factorio.com/blog/post/fff-376): “Discovering new materials,” “Trigger technologies,” and “We can apply this elsewhere.” Original text read; development proposal.
- [FFF #414: Spoils of Agriculture](https://www.factorio.com/blog/post/fff-414): “Spoilage” and “Design journey.” Original text read; pre-release mechanics and author judgments, not transferable balance values.

  application_status: Reference research only; no group playtest or network session performed

# Participation and shared reveals

## Guidance

**Source-supported:** Jackbox's Blather 'Round account describes testing the core mechanic with paper, moving to a digital prototype, and subsequently changing or removing features [1]. The Roomerang diary reports that external feedback changed score communication; internal tests exposed a desire to read elimination reasons and prompted reconsideration of reveal pacing. Its developers also describe repeated work on challenge, advantage, and voting clarity [2]. These are production accounts, not controlled evidence that one social formula succeeds everywhere.

**Reusable planning synthesis:** Specify the social exchange: who creates something, who sees it, who interprets or votes, who benefits, and what becomes public afterward. Record the information held privately and shared by the group at every phase. Plan the opportunities for a quieter player to act, understand the result, and influence the next round; laughter alone does not reveal whose experience is working.

Run the smallest paper version using prompts, slips, and a moderator. Preserve simultaneous or secret decisions when they matter to the mechanic. Use the test to decide whether the exchange creates the intended interaction before adding presentation. Move to devices when private screens, entry friction, input limits, latency, or public display change that interaction.

Structure rounds as understandable instruction, contribution, anticipation, reveal, consequence, and return to participation. Allocate time for reading player-generated content and reactions while limiting passive waiting. Decide whether elimination removes agency, shifts a player's role, or ends the session. Curate prompts for varied familiarity and expression; budget localization and repeated-play variety alongside code and art.

## Applicability and exceptions

Useful for short group games built around communication and interpretation. These sources do not establish hidden-role balance, tournament fairness, moderation policy, or matchmaking design. Friends, strangers, remote callers, and mixed-language groups may behave differently. More rules can enrich strategy but raise onboarding and explanation costs. A successful paper exchange does not verify device usability or online timing.

## Checks

Proposed checks: observe unfamiliar groups without rescuing every rule explanation; record misunderstood phases, contribution distribution, waiting, unread reveals, and disengagement after elimination. Compare scores as understood by players with actual scoring. Test minimum and maximum intended group sizes and one repeat session. These are proposed checks; no group results were fabricated.

## Evidence

1. [McFadden, The Evolution of Blather 'Round](https://www.jackboxgames.com/blog/behind-the-scenes-the-evolution-of-blather-round), 2021-03-19: paper testing introduction and artifacts two through five; full developer article inspected.
2. [Jackbox, Roomerang Dev Diary #3](https://www.jackboxgames.com/blog/behind-the-scenes-of-roomerang-dev-diary-3), 2022-06-27: partner-playtest question and difficult-moment question; developer responses inspected. Linked streams were not watched; two posts supply related studio examples rather than independent research replication.

scope: Asymmetric competitive 1v1 RTS; StarCraft II beta and Legacy of the Void developer accounts
keywords: [RTS, real-time strategy, economy, micro, macro, counterplay, scouting, balance, 실시간전략, 정찰, 견제, 자원, 대응, 밸런스]
status: active

  evidence_check: Two original Blizzard developer articles opened and relevant text read
  application_status: Planning synthesis only; no RTS prototype, replay dataset, or balance test executed
---

# Design RTS pressure through economy, information, and counterplay

## Guidance

**Source observations.** Dustin Browder described initial spreadsheets followed by extensive playtesting, professional input, replays, community feedback, and statistics by race and map. The team removed needless ability steps and retained some defendable rushes. Blizzard's 2015 recap connected starting workers, economy, and diverse micro to pacing; it also described revising the Disruptor's overly binary outcomes.

**Proposed reusable method.** Map the loop as scout → interpret → allocate workers/production → commit → contest → recover. For every aggressive option, write its preparation cost, visible warning, defender response, and recovery opportunity. Specify whether the defender loses through missing information, misinterpreting it, choosing a counter too late, or failing execution. These imply different remedies.

Build a unit-role matrix containing target, effective range, movement, positional dependency, production commitment, and attention demand. Check overlaps before adding units; a new activated ability should create a decision rather than a mandatory click. Attach economy milestones to opportunities for interaction: first scouting, first attack, expansion defense, and composition transition. Changing worker counts or travel distance therefore requires revisiting attack windows and defensive production.

Prototype the smallest two-sided contest with production and a representative map. Keep desired play patterns explicit so equal win rates cannot conceal repetitive openings or unanswerable attacks. Budget unit readability, effects, AI, animation, and map support alongside the number of factions.

## Applicability and exceptions

These sources concern a demanding competitive mode. Campaigns, co-op, controller interfaces, and slower strategic games may prioritize accessibility differently. Asymmetric factions need meaningful options, not identical mechanics or equal clicks. Historical StarCraft values are examples, not transferable constants. The articles describe the developers' process; they do not establish causal proof that a particular change improves every audience's experience.

## Checks

- Replay rush, defensive, and expansion openings on several map geometries; mark warning-to-impact time and available responses.
- Segment outcomes by matchup, map, and skill; inspect replays before diagnosing an aggregate win-rate difference.
- Observe whether players understand a loss and can attempt a different response next match.
- Audit repeated inputs and ability failures for decisions, clarity, and attention overload; separately inspect professional execution limits and newcomer comprehension.

## Evidence

- [StarCraft II Beta Retrospective](https://news.blizzard.com/en-us/article/39345/starcraft-ii-beta-retrospective): Browder's answers on Frenzy, balancing techniques, and rush strategies. Original interview text read; beta-era account.
- [Competitive Multiplayer—Panel Recap](https://news.blizzard.com/en-gb/article/19955452/starcraft-ii-competitive-multiplayer-panel-recap): “Five Goals for the Future,” “Harnessing Player Feedback,” and “More Communication Helps.” Official written 2015 recap read; not a transcript or present-day balance guide.

kind: reference
scope: Turn-based empire and 4X design; Old World designer accounts of Orders and victory conditions

status: active

context:
  evidence_check: Soren Johnson original designer notes opened; body and relevant author comment read
  application_status: Proposed campaign planning method; no simulation, AI evaluation, or full campaign played
---

# Connect turn budgets, empire growth, and campaign endings

## Guidance

**Source observations.** Johnson describes Old World's shared Orders pool as making military and economic actions compete; unlimited movement exposed comprehension problems and led to fatigue. His ending notes connect evolving ambitions to player direction, while victory points provide competitive pressure and a dominance finish. These are his design judgments for Old World, not universal genre findings.

**Proposed reusable method.** For a representative turn, list useful actions, what each consumes, and what must be postponed. Separate genuine allocation decisions from maintenance that is always correct. If introducing a shared action pool, examine empire scale, movement visibility, roads, retreat, and worker tasks together; changing only the command limit can accidentally favor a small mobile force or suppress economic recovery.

Model early, contested middle, dominant, and losing campaign states before expanding the technology tree. For each state, write a plausible goal, a way to change direction, an observable threat, and an end condition. Decide whether victory is a fixed race, a portfolio of objectives, or self-directed simulation. Do not silently switch contracts. For a score finish, check how settlement limits and map size affect score supply. For dynamic objectives, define eligibility, feasibility, replacement, and AI visibility rules.

Treat the ending as a pacing system with production consequences: objective variants, diplomacy explanations, AI behavior, and campaign testing all need authoring time. Prototype a compressed campaign before committing to many eras or factions.

## Applicability and exceptions

Use for empire management with turns and interacting military/economic systems. Small squad tactics, simultaneous turns, and open-ended simulations require different assumptions. Orders are one option; per-unit actions can be appropriate when their local choices remain interesting. Single-player AI may intentionally differ from human victory behavior, but players still need dependable rules for direct interactions. No source establishes a universally correct action cap, campaign length, or victory threshold.

## Checks

- Record meaningful choices and unavoidable maintenance per turn as the empire grows.
- Compare a concentrated offensive, split fronts, and economic investment under the same budget; inspect visibility and recovery.
- Run objective generation against disadvantaged and unusual empire states; flag impossible or repetitive tasks.
- Measure turns between a tester becoming confident of the outcome and the actual ending; inspect surprise losses and dominant-player cleanup separately.

## Evidence

- [Old World Designer Notes #1: Orders](https://www.designer-notes.com/old-world-designer-notes-1-orders/): shared-pool rationale, multiplayer prototypes, visibility, and fatigue; author's July 27 comment on escalating costs. Relevant original text read, not independently replicated.
- [Old World Designer Notes #11: The End](https://www.designer-notes.com/old-world-designer-notes-11-the-end/): victory points, ambitions, AI asymmetry, and double victory. Original designer account read; its genre-wide opinions remain attributed judgments.

  evidence_check: Klei original-authored reprint and two Unknown Worlds original posts opened and read
  application_status: Proposed planning and observation checks; no survival prototype or gameplay test executed
---

# Tie survival pressure and crafting to self-directed exploration

## Guidance

**Source observations.** Klei reports that explicit quests displaced experimentation in an early Don't Starve prototype; recipe presentation and repeated playtests replaced the checklist. Subnautica's early seabases connected exploration outposts with resource costs, power, depth, and hull integrity. Unknown Worlds later acknowledged that its nonlethal survival direction requires readable, reliable mitigation.

**Proposed reusable method.** Map an expedition as notice need or curiosity → prepare → travel and gather → face pressure → return or risk extension → craft capability → choose a new destination. Give each survival meter a purpose in that loop. State which decision it creates, how players replenish it, and whether replenishment remains interesting after mastery. A timer that repeatedly interrupts discovery without creating route or loadout choices is a candidate for revision.

Make a tool-and-resource dependency graph. Tie important upgrades to new access, safer travel, information, or different interactions. Check that the material needed to escape a region's pressure is reachable with earlier capabilities. For shelter, connect site choice to travel, supply, exposure, upkeep, and recovery.

Prototype one complete expedition with temporary presentation. Observe whether players form a second goal without a new explicit quest. Budget biome readability, recipes, creature reactions, and navigation cues together; each new area needs both attraction and a comprehensible way to survive its demands.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies when exploration and player ownership are central. Combat-dominant survival, PvP raiding, multiplayer griefing, and long-term server economies need additional evidence. Klei's quest problem is a specific prototype result, not proof that tutorials or quests are harmful everywhere. Structured objectives can support other experience goals. The 2015 seabase post is historical Early Access behavior. The 2026 letter acknowledges problems and future work; it does not verify that later patches fixed them.

## Checks

- Ask uncoached testers to explain a need, a feasible recipe, and a self-chosen next expedition.
- Trace death or retreat to visible warning, available mitigation, execution, and recoverable loss; distinguish tension from unclear failure.
- Test the shortest valid escape and recovery routes for each resource gate, including lost equipment.
- Track repeated gathering, preparation time, travel, discovery, and abandonment; inspect whether upgrades expand decisions or merely postpone the same chores.

## Evidence

- [Klei: Intrinsic vs. Extrinsic rewards](https://support.klei.com/hc/en-us/articles/360035456011-Intrinsic-vs-Extrinsic-rewards): “Why we implemented quests,” “The unexpected result,” and “Embrace the intrinsic fun.” Klei's preserved developer-authored 2012 account read; not independent psychology verification.
- [Subnautica Seabases Released!](https://unknownworlds.com/en/news/subnautica-seabases-released): base construction, hull integrity, flooding, and power paragraphs. Original March 2015 text read; historical feature example.
- [Subnautica 2 community letter](https://unknownworlds.com/en/news/subnautica-2-community-letter-feedback): May 20, 2026, creature balance and mitigation paragraphs. Original text read; acknowledged limitations and intended improvements only.

# Combine server authority with bounded client prediction

## Guidance

Send player intent to an authoritative simulation; validate it rather than trusting claimed outcomes. Predict latency-sensitive local actions, reconcile against acknowledged server state, then reapply unacknowledged inputs where the movement model supports it. Keep cosmetic smoothing separate from authoritative collision.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Authority reduces some cheats; it does not prevent all cheating. Never grant rewards because a predicted animation played. Current Riot service rates are not adopted as general requirements.

With **client-side prediction**, the player sees a locally simulated input before the authoritative server confirms its result. **Server reconciliation** corrects the local simulation at the acknowledged input and replays newer pending inputs. In a movement system that can replay inputs, force a correction while newer inputs are still pending and check that each pending input affects the resulting state exactly once. [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode)

## Checks

Proposed: inject loss, jitter and invalid inputs; log input sequence, acknowledgement, correction magnitude and authoritative rewards.

## Evidence

- [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode) — 2020 developer account; server authority, prediction and server combat resolution; historical implementation. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V04 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

## Unreal listen-server prediction and reconciliation — 2026-11-20

The local V04 experiment compared waiting and prediction with a separate client and listen server on loopback, sending a shared 20 Hz input sequence via Unreal UDP. Each peer uses 60 ms outgoing lag and 10% loss. The server commits each of the 180 steps once. Both server changes produce the prescribed 48 cm and 64 cm corrections. The replay list is exactly the pending suffix after the acknowledgement. Each run drains to 0 cm final position error.

Input-response median ranges were 145.236–153.142 ms for waiting and 0.498–0.644 ms for prediction across three runs. Client whole-frame p95 was 10.362–11.322 and 10.556–10.673 ms. Use the engine-visible response definition, watch queue depth and measure final convergence independently of transient pose error. This fixture establishes the owned movement contract for one local link with bounded loss and a simple planar motion model.

# Orient image impostors for their intended viewing conditions

## Guidance

A screen-aligned billboard copies the camera's right/up axes. A viewpoint-facing billboard points toward each eye or camera position, projecting an up vector into the plane. An axis-constrained billboard rotates around a fixed axis, often world up for vegetation. When the view lies on that axis there is no unique yaw. Reuse the last valid direction or define an initial fallback.

## Applicability and exceptions

For image impostors and vegetation, a masked material clips texels at a threshold. Translucent blending preserves fractional coverage and can expose ordering problems where planes overlap. Overdraw depends on projected pixel coverage. Shadow behavior depends on the material and render pass.

## Checks

Orbit above and below the plane, roll the camera and mark asymmetric UV corners to catch flips. Compare overlapping layers, solid and cutout shadow samples, stereo views, and GPU time at similar projected coverage.

## Evidence

Godot documents material orientation options, GPU Gems illustrates crossed vegetation cards, and Epic documents blend modes. Earlier Unreal observations covered selected orientations, cutouts and overlaps at achieved frame rates. Requested frame-rate coverage was incomplete. Stereo, general sorting and GPU overdraw remain unmeasured.

- [Godot — Standard Material 3D](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/3d/standard_material_3d.html)

# Choose blend operations in the correct colour space

## Guidance

Straight alpha: C=alpha*S+(1-alpha)*D. Additive commonly accumulates S over D; modulate multiplies them. Use linear-light calculations and distinguish premultiplied from straight-alpha assets. Masked materials discard pixels rather than continuously blending them.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### AlphaComposite

In Unreal's `AlphaComposite` mode the source RGB contribution is already weighted by alpha. Multiplying it again darkens translucent edges. For cutouts, `Opacity Mask Clip Value` sets the threshold for discarding fragments in Masked mode. Compare a partially transparent edge over both light and dark backgrounds, and test the threshold separately.

## Applicability and exceptions

Blend mode affects sorting, depth writes and supported lighting. Adding colour does not make effects readable on all backgrounds.

## Checks

Native scoped dark/bright/HDR,controlled overlap,source-edge andlinear-equation checks appear below;particle/general-sorting/production integration remains unverified.

## Evidence

The original measurements and recordings are held with the historical experiment records.

- [Epic — Material Blend Modes](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/material-blend-modes-in-unreal-engine) — Official documentation; masked, translucent, additive and modulate modes. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V45 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

## Native colour-blending evidence — 2026-11-12

The historical Unreal run adds scoped UE5.8.3 Win64 Development/DX11 straight alpha, premultiplied AlphaComposite, additive and fading modulate.34 final model checks;15 scenes×4 methods×2 actual PIE/package consumers=120 method-case observations,600 foreground and30 absolute background RGB probes;3 matched cost repeats per mode and actual normal1x media. Latest16 limited local sheets/27 without direct prototypes;not full subject certification.

Choose the source representation AND blend contract together. Straight uses aS+(1−a)D. AlphaComposite uses P+(1−a)D,with P already premultiplied in LINEAR light;scale both P and alpha for global fading. Wrong premult-as-straight double-multiplies coverage;straight-as-premult leaks RGB at alpha0. Additive here is D+aS:installed5.8 shader weights emissive by Opacity before hardware1+1. Raw modulate is D×S and ignores Opacity for RGB;to preserve the background at zero coverage,this authored material explicitly uses D×((1−a)+aS),fading toward white. Do not transfer that fade to raw Modulate as an automatic engine feature.

Use texture sRGB according to data meaning. This fixture's numeric-linear32² uncompressed BGRA8 source has RGBbytes204/51/102 and alpha stripes0/64/128/255;its paired premult source rounds linearRGB×alpha.   at the same rate across display cadences.

---
# Combine dead zones, prediction and time-consistent damping

## Guidance

A dead zone lets the subject move inside a screen region before the camera follows. Look-ahead shifts the target toward expected motion. For a target held constant over an interval, update position by `(target - position) * (1 - exp(-lambda * dt))`, added to the current position. `lambda` is a positive decay rate per second. Splitting the same interval preserves the analytical result up to rounding. A damped spring also carries velocity and can model overshoot.

## Applicability and exceptions

For a gameplay camera following a subject, moving targets introduce sampling error. In Unreal Engine, `UCameraComponent` supplies a viewpoint and projection settings. Smoothing its transform still requires explicit camera collision, teleport and framing policies.

## Checks

Compare fixed-target displacement at equal elapsed time under irregular and zero deltas. Then test a fast reversal, obstructed view, teleport and different aspect ratios with a moving subject. Record achieved display cadence.

## Evidence

Keren describes screen framing and Juckett derives spring responses. Earlier Unity and Unreal checks covered stationary-target convergence. They leave moving-target comfort and camera collision open.

- [Itay Keren — Scroll Back](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/scroll-back-the-theory-and-practice-of-cameras-in-side-scrollers)
- [Ryan Juckett — Damped Springs](https://www.ryanjuckett.com/damped-springs/)
- [Epic — UCameraComponent](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/Camera/UCameraComponent?application_version=5.5)

# Implement forgiving platformer input as explicit state windows

## Guidance

Coyote time briefly retains jump eligibility after walking off a ledge. Jump buffering retains an early press until a landing permits it. Timestamp both on the same simulation clock. Give a new press an identity, consume it on successful launch and rearm ground eligibility only on a genuine grounded transition. Decide explicitly whether releasing before landing cancels a request. Corner correction shifts a near-miss clear of geometry and needs its own collision tests.

## Applicability and exceptions

For Unreal Engine 5.8 characters, `ACharacter::CanJumpWhileFalling` permits a subclass to authorize a falling jump under its rules. `ACharacter::Landed` signals contact while movement mode is still Falling. Use `OnMovementModeChanged` when the decision requires the new grounded mode. `ACharacter::OnJumped` reports a jump that actually started.

## Checks

Test the instant before, at and after each expiry, holding and releasing a press, walking off versus jumping off, and landing after an expired press. Count actual launches per request across different measured update rates.

## Evidence

Celeste's creator describes the design techniques. Epic documents the character hooks. Prior Unreal checks covered coyote time and buffering on static platforms. Window lengths, moving platforms and player feel require target-game tests.

- [Maddy Thorson — Celeste & Forgiveness](https://maddymakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_forgiveness/index.html)
- [Maddy Thorson — Celeste & TowerFall Physics](https://www.mattmakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_towerfall_physics/index.html)
- [Epic — ACharacter](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/ACharacter?application_version=5.8)
- [Epic — OnLanded and movement mode](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/ACharacter/OnLanded)

---
# Scale crowd decisions separately from crowd rendering

## Guidance

A spatial hash bins nearby agents for neighbor queries. A shared flow field supplies long-range direction. Local avoidance chooses near-term motion consistent with neighboring bodies. ORCA (Optimal Reciprocal Collision Avoidance) derives velocity constraints under reciprocal avoidance assumptions. A central reservation system instead assigns time and space. In a narrow corridor, a direction lease can admit one stream until bodies clear the exit. The Hitman: Absolution developer slides describe a 2.5D cell map, per-exit evacuation fields, local steering and promotion of selected crowd agents to NPCs.

## Applicability and exceptions

For sustained requests, first-in, first-out (FIFO) preserves admission order but a blocked head can stall the queue. A token bucket limits admission by refill rate and burst size. Backpressure shifts waiting upstream. Reject/drop policies reduce admitted load when loss is allowed. A queue cap bounds that queue alone. A collision-free scene can still deadlock or starve an agent. Keep rendering level of detail (LOD), behavior update frequency and physical safety budgets separate.

If every request must be served, queue rejection violates the service requirement. A finite queue under sustained demand above service capacity needs upstream backpressure, additional capacity or an explicit change to the offered workload. Preserve original request identity and arrival time across backpressure. Compare the oldest waiting request with total completions to expose starvation hidden by aggregate throughput. This conclusion assumes requests continue arriving and each completion consumes capacity. [Reactive Streams — asynchronous backpressure](https://www.reactive-streams.org/)

## Checks

At equal offered demand, retain each request's original arrival time. Measure arrivals, admissions, rejections, oldest age, completions and fraction of all offered work completed on time. Audit swept separation with actual radii, terminal moves and a deliberately conflicting control. Profile whole-frame tails including topology changes, synchronization, animation and rendering on target hardware.

To assess the frame budget, record CPU and GPU timings separately and mark missing GPU samples explicitly.

## Evidence

Fauerby's slides document the historical game architecture. The ORCA authors state their method's assumptions, and Epic documents Mass Avoidance's own spatial and steering model. Earlier grid examples observed both collision-free deadlock and finite queue drain. Finite drains do not prove unlimited progress or native Mass behavior.

- [Kasper Fauerby — Crowds in Hitman: Absolution slides](https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2012/slides/Programming%20Track/Fauerby_Kasper_CrowdsInHitman.pdf)
- [Epic — Mass Avoidance Overview](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/mass-avoidance-overview-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US)
- [ORCA authors](https://gamma-web.iacs.umd.edu/ORCA/)
- [Li et al., corridor symmetry](https://mapf.info/index.php/Main.SKoen20k)
- [LaValle footprint/C-space](https://lavalle.pl/planning/node161.html)
- [fixed-path coordination](https://lavalle.pl/planning/node325.html)
- [RHCR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.07371)
- [PIBT](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.11282)
- [online pickup/delivery](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.10868)

# Distinguish adaptive pacing from hidden outcome control

## Guidance

Use observable performance proxies to choose bounded adjustments: spawn cadence, resources, recovery windows or opponent pace. Separate player-selectable difficulty from dynamic pressure and from competition catch-up. Use hysteresis and cooldowns to avoid rapid oscillation.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Left 4 Dead's Director changes pacing and does not justify arbitrary enemy stat changes. Secretly changing competitive outcomes can undermine trust; no universal tuning coefficients are established.

Dynamic difficulty adjustment (DDA) changes a challenge parameter in response to player performance. **Adaptive dramatic pacing** changes the timing or population of threats to alternate pressure and recovery. Booth describes the latter for Left 4 Dead. For a new design, log the input signal and changed parameter separately. A hysteresis band uses different entry and exit thresholds to prevent rapid reversals near one threshold. [Mike Booth — The AI Systems of Left 4 Dead](https://cdn.fastly.steamstatic.com/apps/valve/2009/ai_systems_of_l4d_mike_booth.pdf)

## Checks

Proposed: log proxy, state, adjustment and reason; compare fixed/adaptive schedules and test manipulation and failure loops.

## Evidence

- [Mike Booth — The AI Systems of Left 4 Dead](https://cdn.fastly.steamstatic.com/apps/valve/2009/ai_systems_of_l4d_mike_booth.pdf) — 2009 developer slides; adaptive pacing, especially pp.79–92. Pacing is not equivalent to arbitrary difficulty scaling. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V41 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

## Unreal fixed and adaptive spawn cadence — 2026-11-20

The local V41 experiment compared fixed eligibility and two-threshold recovery using the same 12-second pressure trace. Cap and stats are held at three concurrent actors, health 100 and attack 10. Recovery enters at pressure 70 and exits at 45 after a minimum of 2.0 seconds. The two windows lasted 2.0 and 2.6 seconds. Both resumed spawning on the release step with a slot available. Fixed spawns totaled 15 and adaptive spawns 12, reproducibly. Whole-frame p95 ranges across three runs were 10.783–12.219 and 10.924–12.222 ms. The actor workloads differ with the policies.

Expire old actors before admitting the next spawn. Keep separate entry and exit thresholds if noisy pressure causes chatter. Quote the measured recovery and cap separately. If queued opportunities would create a resume burst, drop them and restart from a successful spawn. Use an authored trace to verify the policy and playtest the pressure proxy independently of the scheduling logic.

# Choose authored fracture or runtime topology from gameplay needs

## Guidance

For cosmetic damage, authored mesh swaps and debris may be sufficient. For gameplay-relevant fracture, author Geometry Collections, clustering, collision and damage thresholds. Decide separately whether to simulate precomputed pieces or generate new cuts at runtime; use decals for surface marks.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Geometry Collection

In Chaos Destruction, a Geometry Collection represents fracture pieces and their hierarchy. Clustering groups pieces so a group can move together before it breaks. When damage affects traversal, check both the break transition and the resulting collision on the target platform.

## Applicability and exceptions

Pre-fractured does not mean pre-animated. Physics, navigation, replicated state and persistent damage must agree with visual destruction.

## Checks

Bounded native repeated impacts,obstructed pieces,TTL/pool cleanup and active/inactive resets are checked below. Save/load,multiplayer authority and production persistent debris remain unverified.

## Evidence

The original measurements and recordings are held with the historical experiment records.

- [Epic, Destruction Overview](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/destruction-overview) — terminology and applicability of Geometry Collection, Chaos Destruction, clustering. This is source evidence. The suggested target checks remain to be run.

- [Epic — Geometry Collections User Guide](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/geometry-collections-user-guide) — Official documentation; Geometry Collection authoring. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V14 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

# Generate levels from a traversability contract

## Guidance

Binary space partitioning (BSP) recursively splits a region. Place rooms within leaf regions and connect rooms on opposite sides of each internal split. A grid flood fill from the entrance measures reachable floor cells. With unit movement costs, breadth-first search also supplies shortest path lengths for choosing a distant exit. Random walks and authored grammars offer different control over structure.

## Applicability and exceptions

For seeded grid generation, Unreal Engine's `FRandomStream` produces a repeatable sequence from an explicit seed. Reproduction also requires the same draw order, generator and input settings. Epic warns about weak low bits, so use its range functions instead of taking a remainder for bounded choices. A cell connection may still be too narrow for a capsule or impassable under gravity.

## Checks

Repeat a seed, independently flood-fill the final map and sweep the intended capsule along connecting turns and doorways. Test minimum leaf size, narrow corridors, unreachable goals and changes in draw order. Playtest pacing separately.

## Evidence

The PCG chapter describes constructive methods, and Epic documents the seeded stream. Prior Unreal observations covered generated static flat grids and upright capsule clearance. They establish neither full character traversal nor level quality.

- [Shaker et al. — Constructive Generation Methods for Dungeons and Levels](https://www.pcgbook.com/chapter03.pdf)
- [Amit Patel — Introduction to A*](https://www.redblobgames.com/pathfinding/a-star/introduction.html)
- [Epic — FRandomStream](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Core/FRandomStream)
- [Epic — Random Streams](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/random-streams-in-unreal-engine)

# Choose curves by endpoint and timing constraints

## Guidance

For positive duration, clamp elapsed/duration to [0,1], evaluate a curve and interpolate the endpoints. Linear interpolation keeps speed constant. `smoothstep` uses 3t²−2t³ with zero endpoint slopes. Perlin's quintic `smootherstep` also has zero second derivatives at the ends. For a CSS-style cubic Bézier timing function solve x(u)=t before using y(u). Asymmetric control points reveal mistakes that a linear curve hides.

## Applicability and exceptions

For a finite tween, pause can freeze the cursor, restart can take the current displayed value as its new start, and reversal can traverse the same cursor backwards. Restart can change velocity and reversal can flip it. For Unreal component motion, `SetWorldLocation` publishes a world-space transform. A swept root can stop before the requested position if collision blocks it.

## Checks

Test endpoints and overshoot, zero duration, pause/resume, restart and reverse mid-curve or after completion. Check single completion delivery, sample the actual component transform separately from the solver value, and distinguish elapsed wall time from synthetic samples.

## Evidence

W3C specifies Bézier timing and Perlin derives the quintic polynomial. Earlier collisionless component checks observed position-continuous restart, curve reversal and small transform rounding differences. Some requested display rates were missed. Collision, maintained velocity and player feel require separate checks.

- [W3C — CSS Easing Functions Level 1](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-easing-1/)
- [Ken Perlin — Improving Noise](https://mrl.cs.nyu.edu/~perlin/paper445.pdf)
- [Epic — Set World Location](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/USceneComponent/K2_SetWorldLocation?application_version=5.8)

# Budget dense vegetation across geometry and pixel cost

## Guidance

Use crossed alpha-tested cards or meshes according to silhouette importance. Cull outside useful visibility, reduce distant detail, batch compatible instances, and animate vertices with spatially coherent wind. Keep draw submission, vertex cost, overdraw and shadow cost as separate budgets.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Static Mesh Foliage

Unreal's Static Mesh Foliage uses hardware instancing to submit repeated meshes in batches. Overdraw is a different cost: overlapping foliage cards can shade the same screen region repeatedly. Vary card coverage at fixed instance count and inspect GPU time separately from draw submissions.

## Applicability and exceptions

Instancing reduces submission overhead, not all shading work. Cards can cost more pixels than denser opaque geometry. These historical sources do not certify Nanite foliage behavior.

## Checks

Actual static patch instancing/culling,alpha-hole/two-sided/boundary/reentry evidence appears below. Wind/shadows/LOD and isolated overdraw pass costs remain unverified.

## Evidence

The original measurements and recordings are held with the historical experiment records.

- [Epic, Foliage Mode](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/foliage-mode-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US) — terminology and applicability of Static Mesh Foliage, hardware instancing, overdraw. This is source evidence. The suggested target checks remain to be run.

- [Kurt Pelzer — Rendering Countless Blades of Waving Grass](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems/part-i-natural-effects/chapter-7-rendering-countless-blades-waving-grass) — GPU Gems ch.7; crossed quads and animation. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Tiago Sousa — Vegetation Procedural Animation and Shading in Crysis](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems3/part-iii-rendering/chapter-16-vegetation-procedural-animation-and-shading-crysis) — GPU Gems 3 ch.16; wind animation. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V19 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

## Native instancing and culling evidence — 2026-11-13

The historical Unreal run adds scoped UE5.8.3 Win64 Development/DX11: 37 native policy checks, 47 evidence tests, four actual PIE and four packaged behavior worlds, twelve matched cost players and normal1x native media. Latest coverage is 20 limited sheets /23 without direct prototypes. This verifies a small static diagnostic patch, not a whole foliage system; previous missed FPS bands remain unverified.

Use one shared mesh/material for repeated static grass clumps and compare component/submission overhead separately from masked screen coverage. Here one clump is two perpendicular vertical cards (8 vertices /4 triangles), oneLOD/nonNanite, collision/shadows off, local64×96cm bounds centred at0. Actual instance originZ48 puts the base at0. Behavior uses16 clumps, cost1024, with deterministic positions/yaw. Every actual component or ISM instance matrix and shared mesh/material binding is independently checked. Two owned assets hold a linear64×64BGRA8 point/clamp/noMip numeric blade mask and Masked/Unlit/TwoSided material with ISM support; all4096source texels and1344opaque texels match independent values. No realistic lighting/art quality claim.

Do not assume equal numeric cull distances imply equal visible coverage. Installed5.8 default CPU primitive distance uses its bounding sphere edge (r.DistanceCullToSphereEdge=1); instance draw-distance uses its centre. Keep renderer settings unchanged and map the authored1000cm centre cutoff to individual max=1000−actual bounds radius and ISMend1000. The crossed-card vertex sphere radius is57.6888204cm (sqrt(32²+48²)), not the AABB-corner radius. Record actual component bounds/cached limits and effective scalability;rederive after mesh bounds,scale or engine changes. Exact equality1000 is visible under the checked float predicates;1001/1300 disappear and999 reappears using the SAME live component/instance. Hard culling can pop;fade/LOD behavior is outside this result.

Correctness combines native geometry/material/UV/section/metadata/placement/cull settings/capture membership with actual linear-HDR pixels. Independent projection,D3D11eight-fractional-bit raster snapping,perspectiveUV and point-alpha sampling determine expected depth/colour. Front/back patches contain observed pixels from both winding orientations;single-clump blade/hole probes distinguish cutout from opaque cards. Four arms ×eight cases in actualPIE and packaged worlds match;maximumRGBerror0.000484452 stays below the unchanged.008 bound. Display screenshots are tone-mapped1280×720, not the numeric640×512 observer. Expected1024OFF/182ON centre-distance survivors are not GPU-visible counts:frustum and occlusion also apply. Sparse cost probes complement full placement/settings and behavior evidence;they do not prove every hidden instance's pixels.

Matched cost:three sequential packaged runs perarm (individual/ISM ×cullingOFF/ON),same1024placements/mesh/material/camera/config/executable,1280×720,2s warmup+8s measurement. Only the chosen representation is allocated;1024components versus1ISM is the intended representation difference. Setup/registration/GPU calibration,observer readback,finalsave/exit are outside measurement;HUD/scalar arrays remain included. Per-frame declared build/read deltas are zero, with no measured camera/placement/cull updates. Default dynamic batching stays enabled,not forced off for a favorable result. individual-off: whole wall p95 9.984–10.222 ms; whole-RHI draw medians [865, 865, 865]. ism-off: whole wall p95 9.390–9.666 ms; whole-RHI draw medians [27, 27, 27]. individual-on: whole wall p95 9.546–9.942 ms; whole-RHI draw medians [149, 149, 149]. ism-on: whole wall p95 9.621–9.737 ms; whole-RHI draw medians [27, 27, 27]. Whole wall/cadence,delayed wholeGPU and whole-RHI draw samples are distinct from isolated foliage passes or pairedCPU/GPUframes. Host load is uncontrolled;no universal speedup/production budget or isolated alpha-overdraw claim. Setup/memory/update costs were not separately benchmarked.

One fresh review exposed unvalidated recorded cvars and execution environment. Five negative cases first failed, then the checker required all four unchanged renderer/scalability values,consumer/mode/required arguments,actual5.8.3/D3D11 log and the final matrix. Required artifact hashes reject missing or substituted data. No renderer settings or general verification framework changed. First package compilation also caught editor-only NaniteSettings access:guard the assignment and inspect actual runtime HasValidNaniteData();both final builds and native runs pass. Failed GPU reads return failed audit evidence before array indexing. Details and scope are in the linked report.

Adopt when repeated static clumps make component/submission overhead significant, while validating actual masked density/view angles/GPU costs. This prototype excludes LOD,wind,Nanite,large forests/streaming,collision/interaction,lit shadows and renderer/tool improvements. Prefer a locally profiled content choice;instancing does not remove all pixel work. [Epic ISM documentation](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/instanced-static-mesh-component-in-unreal-engine) supports grouping repeats;the rolling5.9 page is not a5.8LOD guarantee. Installed5.8 SceneVisibility/InstanceCulling and actual outputs establish this cull mapping. GPU Gems source mechanisms below are historical,not current hardware performance promises. No commit/push.

Measured adoption judgment: whole-RHI draw medians fell from865 individual/OFF and149 individual/ON to27 in both ISM arms. Whole-frame p95 gains were modest;adding culling to ISM did not establish a consistent additional gain in this small static scene. Use actual frame/GPU evidence,not only expected distance-survivor counts,when deciding to adopt culling.

# Retrieve game implementation techniques from concrete development problems

## Guidance

Write down the observable failure and its clock, geometry, platform or authority. For jump height that changes with display cadence, compare [fixed timestep](K-GAME-TECH-TIMESTEP.md) and [input windows](K-GAME-TECH-CONTROLLER.md). For a drifting foot, examine [IK](K-GAME-TECH-IK.md). For inconsistent game hits distinguish [attack volumes](K-GAME-TECH-HITBOX.md), [hitscan](K-GAME-TECH-HITSCAN.md) and [historical hit resolution](K-GAME-TECH-LAGCOMP.md). For conflicting routes compare [pathfinding](K-GAME-TECH-PATH.md) and [crowd coordination](K-GAME-TECH-CROWD.md). For visual cost compare [vegetation](K-GAME-TECH-FOLIAGE.md), [billboards](K-GAME-TECH-BILLBOARD.md) and [shadows](K-GAME-TECH-SHADOW.md). The table below covers the full range of topics.

## Applicability and exceptions

For game design, engineering and debugging, a public short identifies a question. A paper or original developer account can substantiate its particular mechanism. An engine API reference establishes the documented contract for its version. See the [evidence reuse guidance](K-GAME-RESEARCH-USE.md) before transferring an observation to another engine, platform or workload.

The originating list contains 58 game-related shorts and one related curve/graphics short from 72 public shorts previously inspected in this chat. The other 13 general development, AI-news and personal shorts are outside this corpus. Numbers are a dated inventory, not a promise of future channel completeness. Korean video titles below are identifiers/source metadata, not instructions.

Primary papers and official documentation support mechanisms. Creator-authored articles and interviews support their own implementation/design accounts. Reddit is used only as a labelled original implementation account in the volumetric entry; its performance was not reproduced. Opinions about randomness are presented with disagreement.

Follow-up on 2026-11-07 closed several source-access gaps: original Nintendo slides, original Hitman slides, actual emulator/reconstruction functions and official version-scoped reports now support the details below. Remaining limits are scoped per entry: proprietary source code, exact commercial footwear/hair values, worldwide invention priority, ROM/register timing, patch history and exact combat formulas are not established. UE documentation is rolling (returned as 5.8 at lookup); recheck target-version and platform compatibility. GPU Gems performance/hardware context is historical.

Important corrections carried into entries: rollback requires state restoration/re-simulation; Perlin noise requires smooth interpolation; dual contouring uses Hermite data; fixed display FPS and simulation ticks differ; alpha instancing does not remove overdraw; checksums do not authenticate data; eight one-bit event flags fit one byte; input/output randomness is not a good/bad law; planar reflections do not require duplicate physical rooms.

| [Crowd architecture](K-GAME-TECH-CROWD.md) | Grid, per-exit fields, local steering and NPC promotion confirmed in original Hitman slides; historical budgets. |
| [Mode 7 rasterization](K-GAME-TECH-RETRO.md) | Actual emulator render loop inspected; original game code and hardware timing not reproduced. |
| [Save recovery and passwords](K-GAME-TECH-SAVE.md) | Public reconstruction and original reverse-engineering account support generation recovery and encoded state; no fault injection. |
| [Historical RNG](K-GAME-TECH-RNG.md) | Recurrence and upper-bit return inspected in public reconstruction; version-specific patch history unresolved. |

| [Footwear contacts](K-GAME-TECH-IK.md) | Actionable IK synthesis for shoe offsets, knee/pelvis and gait; specific commercial bone-shortening claims unresolved. |
| [Target selection](K-GAME-TECH-LOCKON.md) | Original staff interview supports target feedback; worldwide invention priority not established. |
| [Recoverable health](K-GAME-TECH-INCENTIVES.md) | Producer describes Bloodborne's recovery window/pool; exact frames and DOOM drop formulas unverified. |
| [Interactive smoke](K-GAME-TECH-VOLUME.md) | Dated Valve patch notes establish volume interactions; complete solver and replication remain unpublished here. |
| [Controller-driven ACE](K-GAME-TECH-MEMORY.md) | Hardware replay project's own artifact inspected; exact vulnerability trigger not reproduced. |

## Checks

Read the entry's applicability and primary evidence. Choose a representative scene, a counterexample and an observable acceptance condition. Measure actual update rate, frame cost or gameplay outcome as appropriate. Keep target-specific tuning, raw run output and adoption decisions in the game project.

## Evidence

The original measurements and recordings are held with the historical experiment records.

The historical discovery map below links public videos to the corresponding references. Versioned API documentation, creator accounts and algorithms are cited within the individual entries. Representative primary sources include the following implementation accounts and derivations.

- [Glenn Fiedler — Fix Your Timestep!](https://gafferongames.com/post/fix_your_timestep/)
- [Maddy Thorson — Celeste & Forgiveness](https://maddymakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_forgiveness/index.html)
- [Kurt Pelzer — Rendering Countless Blades of Waving Grass](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems/part-i-natural-effects/chapter-7-rendering-countless-blades-waving-grass)
- [Glenn Fiedler — Deterministic Lockstep](https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/)

## Source inventory and local follow-up

External research checked 2026-11-07. This inventory records access boundaries; it does not copy full articles, transcripts or papers. The channel and short metadata/transcripts were inspected in the preceding chat turn. Sources below are deduplicated; entry-level citations describe the relevant claim.

- [Microsoft — Cascaded Shadow Maps](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/dxtecharts/cascaded-shadow-maps) — Technical article; frustum partitioning and stability sections.

- [Epic — Planar Reflections](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/planar-reflections-in-unreal-engine) — Official documentation; SSR comparison and rendering cost.

- [Kurt Pelzer — Rendering Countless Blades of Waving Grass](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems/part-i-natural-effects/chapter-7-rendering-countless-blades-waving-grass) — GPU Gems ch.7; crossed quads and animation.
- [Tiago Sousa — Vegetation Procedural Animation and Shading in Crysis](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems3/part-iii-rendering/chapter-16-vegetation-procedural-animation-and-shading-crysis) — GPU Gems 3 ch.16; wind animation.

# Choose hair representation and simulation independently

## Guidance

Use cards for authored silhouette and lower representation budgets; consider strands for close detailed characters. Separate render representation, simulation guides, collision and LOD. Establish platform support before selecting a groom pipeline.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

Epic documents separate guide simulation, interpolation onto render strands, hair-density voxelization, visibility, lighting and composition passes. Lower render-curve count and lower guide count target different costs; even invisible hair can require updates for shadows. Use per-pass profiling before choosing cards/strands or decimation. Capcom's CEDEC 2026 session confirms a PRAGMATA long-hair/strand workflow in Maya and RE ENGINE, but its accessible description does not verify exact strand/point counts.

### Groom Asset

A Groom Asset can carry guide curves and render strands. With strand interpolation, simulated guides drive the motion of render strands. A guide-count change and a render-curve change affect different work. Profile the relevant passes at the intended LOD before selecting a reduction.

## Applicability and exceptions

Not every rendered strand needs independent simulation. Long hair difficulty depends on representation, collision and animation requirements; short hair is not a universal technical necessity.

## Checks

Proposed: test shoulders, fast turns, headgear and LOD changes; profile several characters together.

## Evidence

- [Epic — Hair Rendering and Simulation](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/hair-rendering-and-simulation-in-unreal-engine) — Overview text only; strands and cards. No platform-specific budget verified. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V13 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [Epic — Groom Scalability and Performance](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/groom-scalability-and-performance-with-unreal-engine) — Geometry scalability and pipeline stages text inspected; no project performance measured. Checked 2026-11-07.
- [Capcom speakers — PRAGMATA hair and facial development](https://cedec.cesa.or.jp/2026/timetable/detail/s69f305ca68328/) — Official CEDEC 2026 description and speaker affiliations; detailed presentation assets/counts not accessed. Checked 2026-11-07.

## Unreal masked cards and Groom strands — 2026-11-20

The local V13 experiment used one 19-lock style with 722 card triangles and 3,192 Groom curves. Rigid scalp attachment, fixed LOD and simulation off isolate the two representations under common lights and camera poses. A matched no-hair render provides silhouette and background gap evidence. Both have visible gaps in the tested checker backdrop. Near-side strand coverage was 3.68 times card coverage for these assets. Read the pose-level overlap and backdrop samples for this hairstyle.

Three runs yielded whole-frame p95 of 12.456–14.736 ms (cards) and 9.925–12.799 ms (strands), and whole-GPU p95 of 13.708–14.826 and 9.808–13.885 ms respectively. Use the screen footprint at actual camera distances and keep the light and material setup comparable. GPU totals cover the complete selected representation. Add separate skinning, collision and LOD evidence when those systems are active.

# Keep attack, hurt and physical collision contracts distinct

## Guidance

A hitbox describes an active attack volume, a hurtbox describes a damage-receiving region, and movement collision constrains the character body. Give an attack its own identity and keep a set of already affected targets for that activation. For fast linear motion sweep the previous-to-current volume only over the part of the update interval where the attack is active. Test initial overlap explicitly where the rules permit it.

## Applicability and exceptions

In Unreal Engine, `UWorld::SweepMultiByChannel` collects touches up to the nearest blocker on the selected channel. Its Boolean return indicates a blocking hit. Inspect the `FHitResult` array even when the Boolean is false, since touch-only results can still be relevant. A straight sphere sweep approximates only a linear trajectory. Rotating limbs or moving targets may require additional sampling.

## Checks

Check a complete crossing between frames, just-before/open/close boundaries, a wall preceding a hurtbox, an owner touch and repeated hits from one activation. Verify the touch-only case independently. For hitstop, specify which clocks pause and whether input buffering continues.

## Evidence

Epic documents the query contract and collision responses. Thorson describes collision and forgiveness in specific games. Prior Unreal observations covered linear sphere paths and static upright targets. They do not establish animation-driven contact, hitstop, networking or combat feel.

- [Epic SweepMultiByChannel](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/Engine/UWorld/SweepMultiByChannel?application_version=5.5)
- [collision response reference](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/collision-response-reference-in-unreal-engine)
- [Maddy Thorson — Celeste & Forgiveness](https://maddymakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_forgiveness/index.html)
- [Maddy Thorson — Celeste & TowerFall Physics](https://www.mattmakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_towerfall_physics/index.html)

  unobstructed path. Define starting overlaps and ownership filtering.

status: active

---
# Resolve hitscan from aiming intent and muzzle constraints

## Guidance

In a game with an offset third-person camera, the camera ray chooses aim intent. Query the path from the in-game emitter to that point against the gameplay collision world. A camera peeking past cover can see a target while the emitter remains obstructed. Define whether a blocked or initially embedded origin cancels the action. Cosmetic tracers can display the resolved result.

## Applicability and exceptions

For Unreal object-type queries, `UWorld::LineTraceSingleByObjectType` casts a segment against selected object types. `UWorld::OverlapAnyTestByObjectType` checks whether a shape already overlaps a selected type at a pose. Configure ownership, object filters and the authoritative pose consistently. In Unity 3D, `Physics.Raycast` misses the collider containing its origin, so an overlap test can be needed there.

## Checks

Compare unobstructed and occluded poses, an emitter inside geometry, a body-to-emitter path crossing cover, ignored owner and obstacles beyond the intended endpoint. Exercise actual collision responses and authoritative animation timing in the target game.

## Evidence

Epic and Unity document the respective query APIs. Riot describes server-side decisions in VALORANT. Earlier simple static collider checks in Unity and Unreal exposed camera-only false positives. Animated targets, latency and server reconciliation were outside those checks.

- [Unity 6000.0 — Physics.Raycast](https://docs.unity.com/en-us/engine/6000.0/script-reference/unityengine/physics/raycast)
- [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode)
- [Epic — UWorld object-type queries](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/UWorld?application_version=5.8)

# Fit animation contacts with constrained inverse kinematics

## Guidance

Inverse kinematics (IK) solves joint transforms for a desired endpoint or effector. Unreal's Two Bone IK uses a three-joint limb and a Joint Target Location to guide the bend. FABRIK iteratively reaches backward and forward through a chain, with a precision goal and iteration budget. Express the effector, pole and support plane in the solver's declared spaces. Offset the ankle by the calibrated sole or shoe geometry.

## Applicability and exceptions

For static supports, latch a stance contact and release it during swing. Blend from the source pose, then solve pelvis displacement against that blended endpoint and both legs' reach. Fall back if a compatible pelvis shift does not exist. `UPoseableMeshComponent` allows bone-space or component/world pose operations according to the API used. Specify the supported space for each operation. Keep visual contact independent of the gameplay capsule.

## Checks

Compare source and final skinned poses on flat ground, stairs, conflicting contacts, mid-blend, unreachable supports and swing release. Verify heel/toe penetration, knee continuity, previous-frame pose history and unchanged gameplay collision.

## Evidence

Epic documents the solve controls and poseable mesh APIs. Prior Unreal observations covered a static support, calibrated sampling and reach rejection for a skinned biped. Commercial footwear rigs, moving supports, retargeting and a production animation graph were outside that observation.

- [Epic — Two Bone IK](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/animation-blueprint-two-bone-ik-in-unreal-engine)
- [Epic — FABRIK AnimBP Node](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/fabrik-animation-blueprint-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US)
- [Epic — UPoseableMeshComponent](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/UPoseableMeshComponent?application_version=5.8)

# Evaluate rewards by the behavior they make worthwhile

## Guidance

Proposed design synthesis: define the intended repeated choice, its time/resource cost, failure consequence and alternative. Efficiency upgrades or automation can change the work rather than only increasing numbers. Attack-to-heal and time pressure can reward engagement, but compare them against safe policies and different skill levels. Consult the existing economy and combat entries before tuning.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

Bloodborne producer Masaaki Yamagiwa explicitly describes a short post-hit window in which counterattacks restore part of the lost health, with recoverable health visible in the gauge. A transferable implementation tracks a bounded recoverable-damage pool and expiry, then applies eligible hits without exceeding it; renewal/stacking rules must be authored rather than inferred. This differs from ordinary kill-to-heal. DOOM's GDC presentation is an authoritative discovery lead for push-forward combat, but its description alone does not establish exact resource/drop formulas.

## Applicability and exceptions

Bloodborne's short recovery window is supported by a producer account; exact damage percentages/timing and DOOM drop algorithms remain separate questions. Rewards are not proof of enjoyment or a reason to ban cautious play.

Bloodborne's **Regain System** makes a portion of newly lost health recoverable for a limited time through eligible counterattacks. When adapting this as a risk/reward mechanic, check whether players can read the recoverable portion and whether repeated hits can restore more than the recorded loss. Retain the expiry and recovery cap as game-specific choices. [Masaaki Yamagiwa — Bloodborne combat details](https://blog.playstation.com/2014/08/13/bloodborne-on-ps4-new-combat-details/)

## Checks

Proposed: compare passive/aggressive/efficient policies, record time-to-upgrade and recovery opportunities, then test perceived agency.

## Evidence

- Reuse [resource-flow analysis](K-GAME-ECONOMY.md) for upgrade/automation costs and [combat response design](K-GAME-ACTION-COMBAT.md) for offensive incentives. These existing references retain their source-specific conditions; no new playtest is implied.

- [Ian Schreiber — Probability and Randomness](https://gamebalanceconcepts.wordpress.com/2010/07/28/level-4-probability-and-randomness/) — 2010 designer course; probability distributions and expected values. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V05, V17, V34 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [Masaaki Yamagiwa — Bloodborne combat details](https://blog.playstation.com/2014/08/13/bloodborne-on-ps4-new-combat-details/) — Producer-authored Regain System discussion; historical 2014 design account, not exact frame/percentage data. Checked 2026-11-07.
- [id Software/GDC — Embracing Push Forward Combat](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2KQNpQD8Ayo) — 2018 official talk description only; detailed formulas not verified. Checked 2026-11-07.

## Designing timed counterattack recovery

Define recoverable health from actual applied damage. Use separate expiry times when later damage should leave the earlier deadline in place. If the deadline is exclusive, expire the affected amounts before resolving hits at that tick. Limit recovery by the live health deficit, remaining recoverable amount and a per-hit cap. Count a confirmed hit only once.

Show current and recoverable health as distinct gauge segments. Compare immediate engagement, delayed engagement and retreat under representative exposure. The health advantage depends on how much the counterattack recovers and how much extra damage it exposes the player to.

# Fake inaccessible interiors with ray-box mapping

## Guidance

Use on windows whose rooms cannot be entered. Transform the view ray into room-local coordinates, intersect an implicit room box, and sample the corresponding wall/floor/ceiling texture. Introduce room variation without authoring full interior meshes.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### interior mapping

Interior mapping uses a ray-box intersection in room-local coordinates to determine which interior surface appears behind a facade. Check grazing views and nearby cameras where parallax errors or the lack of real geometry become visible.

## Applicability and exceptions

The room is a shading illusion: it does not automatically provide real collision, navigation, shadows or interactive furniture. Close grazing views reveal limits.

## Checks

Native camera, edge/facade, transform, UV and epsilon checks are summarized below. Actual FPS bands, entry, lighting, glass, furniture and streaming remain unverified.

## Evidence

The original measurements and recordings are held with the historical experiment records.

- [Joost van Dongen — Interior Mapping](https://www.proun-game.com/Oogst3D/CODING/InteriorMapping/InteriorMapping.pdf) — 2008 author manuscript; abstract and ray intersections. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V15 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

## Native ray-box mapping evidence — 2026-11-12

The historical Unreal run provides scoped Win64 Development/DX11 evidence: 48 policy/atlas/time/mesh metadata checks, 50 evidence tests, 576 actual window observations / 14,400 candidate-reference pixel pairs and three matched packaged cost runs per representation. **Verification is partial: final actual 30/60/144 FPS bands were not reached and remain unverified.** Scripted time inputs and normal 1x capture do not establish actual FPS. Latest coverage: 18 limited sheets / 25 without direct prototypes.

Implementation order: transform camera to room-local space → undo aperture UV mirror to recover the physical front point → normalize the inward ray → find the nearest box exit plane → calculate face UV → apply intended texture mirror. Positive nonuniform scale, rotation and translation are checked; UV mirroring is not negative geometry scale. Authored bounds: finite UV [0,1], Mirror 0/1, sizes 1..10000 cm, absolute camera components ≤1e7, CameraZ>1e-4. Reject Dz≥-1e-6; skip axes with absolute direction ≤1e-6; resolve ties X/Y/Z. CPU invalid input preserves output; GPU returns finite black. These are local bounds, not source-game values or large-coordinate certification.

Four 180×140 cm windows use depths 100/160/220/280 cm. The shared 320×64 atlas contains five 64² gradient/checker faces; all 20,480 actual source texels are independently checked. It is numeric linear BGRA8, point/clamp, LOD0, no mips, uncompressed. Materials are single-sided Opaque/Unlit. Apertures use two triangles / one section; actual five-wall rooms use ten triangles / **five sections per room**. Shared facade: 21 rectangles / 42 triangles. The actual-geometry reference is collisionless and does not verify entry, physics or navigation.

Eighteen cases cover 12 normal camera/transform/mirror/depth cases and six wrong/invalid/synthetic epsilon controls. Actual moving/stable audits use 5×5 points including .01/.99 borders. Independent calculations bind native mesh, UV, winding, sections, parameters, camera/projection, visibility and capture lists to pixel-center rays and source texels. Pixel rounding can move an intended window point onto the facade: 161 final pairs were classified accordingly. Maximum RGB error .006832682 remains below the unchanged .008 bound. Linear HDR numerical evidence differs from tone-mapped screenshots. Synthetic camera uniforms test near-zero XY and near-parallel Z input/output, not physical views or GPU instruction counts.

Adoption checks learned here: use actual recorded camera axes within a justified native orientation bound; account for DX11 1/256-pixel vertex snapping and perspective UV at point-texel boundaries separately for each representation; reject handled runtime ensures even when pixels and exit code pass; initialize owned runtime mesh UV density metadata. The real failures, RED→GREEN regressions and final reruns are linked in the results. Initial black/equal captures were rejected and repaired with per-view component lists. One fresh review found missing edge/epsilon coverage, which was repaired without a repeated review. All final native consumers use the corrected v2 executable; older higher-FPS runs cannot certify it.

At 1280×720, four static windows, two seconds warmup and eight seconds measurement, whole-wall p95 ranges across three runs were: flat 10.406–10.745 ms; mapping 10.517–10.890 ms; actual geometry 10.493–10.562 ms. Measurement excludes captures, readback, mesh builds, transforms, MID updates and final save/exit; HUD/scalar arrays remain included. Setup and GPU calibration precede warmup. Whole wall/cadence and delayed whole-GPU counters are distinct from isolated shader cost. Ranges overlap; the five-section geometry baseline is unoptimized, host scheduling was uncontrolled, and four windows do not establish a universal speedup or city budget. Moving-camera MID update cost was not isolated.

Choose mapping for inaccessible decorative planar windows needing box-like parallax; choose flat textures if parallax adds no value; construct real spatial content for entry, collision, navigation, furniture or close interaction. Lighting, glass, reflections, filtering/mips, curved facades, negative geometry scale, arbitrary building corners, streaming and large coverage need separate validation. Recheck actual assets, view angles, lifecycle and frame tails. No renderer/framework expansion or publication occurred.

Source: [Joost van Dongen's 2018 author article](https://joostdevblog.blogspot.com/2018/09/interior-mapping-real-rooms-without.html), checked 2026-11-12, supports ray/plane/UV mechanics and regular-box/furniture limits. Today's direct 2008 PDF fetch timed out; its historical 2026-11-07 check remains separate. Old float4 optimization and UE4 function availability were not assumed for UE5.8. Installed engine APIs plus actual builds establish this authored path.

# Bound historical hit resolution for latency compensation

## Guidance

Retain timestamped collision history and resolve eligible shots against a bounded reconstructed past view. Account for clock mapping and interpolation delay. Keep the authoritative current world distinct from temporary historical queries.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Rewinding benefits a shooter's historical view but can hurt a player who has reached cover. 'People always prefer this' is not established; choose fairness rules and maximum rewind explicitly.

With **server rewind**, an authoritative game evaluates an eligible input against stored historical collision poses. The target time depends on how client time maps to server time and how far the displayed remote state lagged behind. Clamp it to available history, choose a missing-history policy, and verify the live collision world is unchanged after a historical query. Riot's account supplies a game-specific example of the latency tradeoff. [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode)

## Checks

Proposed: record both client views and server history for cover transitions, fast targets and asymmetric latency.

## Evidence

- [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode) — 2020 developer account; server authority, prediction and server combat resolution; historical implementation. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V28 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

## Unreal listen-server rewind — 2026-11-20

The local V28 experiment used one moving box, fixed cover, a separate client, 60 ms outbound emulation in both directions and matched server decision ticks. Across three repetitions per profile, all-offered client-view agreement was 12/36 current and 28/36 rewind. Rewind agreed on all 28 evaluated shots, rejected the four out-of-range and four missing-sample requests, and accepted eight hits at 50 or 200 ms after entering cover. Both profiles suppressed four duplicates and preserved current collision in 40 before/after checks per session.

Active decision p95 ranged from 2.200–2.500 µs for current and 3.500–14.600 µs for rewind. Host whole-frame p95 was 29.605–29.934 ms and 29.570–29.656 ms respectively. Client whole-frame p95 was 8.551–9.017 ms and 8.602–9.134 ms.

Map requests to the exact displayed pose time, then apply an explicit age limit and missing-history disposition. For fixed occluders, cap the historical geometry query at the first static hit. Use distinct collision representations so a historical query cannot change current collisions. Count explicit refusals in the all-offered rate and record time behind cover as a separate outcome. Measure decision work along with server and client frames. The 400 ms limit, exact sample matching and one-box result belong to this workload. A game with moving occluders or skeletal collision needs its own history representation and policy.
